PAIR：面向LinkedIn高级人才推荐的偏好对齐意图检索

9/10
PAIR: Preference-Aligned Intent Retrieval for Premium People Recommendation at LinkedIn
Zhongkai Shangguan, Yanbin Kang, Yihan Cao, Ruijie Jiang, Qiannan Yin, Chang Yue...
核心总结:

论文针对职业平台中基于画像相似性检索错排候选的问题，提出PAIR框架：将直接偏好优化的偏好学习视角实例化为曝光条件多负例对比目标，并聚合带位置编码的点击历史以刻画用户意图，实现无需参考模型、可常数开销更新用户表示的高效意图检索。

个性化推荐理由:

将DPO偏好学习改造为曝光条件多负例对比检索目标，并融合点击历史的位置编码实现高效用户意图建模，直接且新颖地服务于推荐召回，核心贡献突出。

2026-10-22 08:57:58 | arXiv:2610.30941v1 |
cs.IRcs.LG
查看完整摘要
General-purpose embeddings trained on profile similarity often misrank candidates in professional networks, where relevance is inherently complementary rather than symmetric. For example, job seekers usually engage with recruiters rather than peers with similar credentials. Current user recommendation systems are often built on profile similarity or social signals such as shared connections, which are effective in friend-oriented social networks where users tend to connect with similar people or within existing social circles. However, this does not hold in professional platforms like LinkedIn, where interactions are often goal-driven and cross-role interactions, requiring matching based on complementary intents rather than similarity or social proximity. In this paper, we propose Preference-Aligned Intent Retrieval, a retrieval framework that is designed to model user intent preference instead of profile similarity. Our approach is inspired by the preference-learning perspective of Direct Preference Optimization, but instantiates preference learning as an exposure-conditioned multi-negative contrastive objective over embeddings. This formulation simplifies training and scales efficiently to large candidate sets without requiring an explicit reference model. Furthermore, we incorporate user interaction history by aggregating click histories with positional encoding to capture temporal order, which enables constant-cost updates of user representations over a fixed history window, allowing new interactions to be incorporated without re-encoding history. As a result, personalization can be updated efficiently using lightweight CPU operations without repeated LLM inference during serving. We evaluate PAIR on LinkedIn's Premium retrieval task. A 0.6B parameter model achieves a MAR@100 of 0.45.

OneLatent：面向高效基础推荐模型的潜在推理

9/10
OneLatent: Latent Reasoning for Efficient Foundation Recommendation Models
Hao Jiang, Peiru Du, Pengfei Yao, Mengting Li, Siyuan Lou, Kuo Cai, Sheng Yu, Qi...
核心总结:

针对显式CoT推理在基础推荐模型中推理开销大且依赖人工模板的问题，提出OneLatent框架：先通过多视图自适应CoT生成多样化的教师监督信号，再以三阶段潜在token对齐将推理痕迹内化为可学习token，最后用多阶段课程后训练激活潜在推理能力，实现无需生成冗长推理痕迹的高效推荐。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文将LLM的显式CoT推理压缩为可学习的潜在token，直接应用于基础推荐模型，属于LLM在推荐系统中的直接应用创新，且涉及工业级部署，高度契合关注方向。

2026-10-22 03:39:38 | arXiv:2610.30675v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong reasoning capabilities, motivating their use as the backbone of foundation recommendation models (FRMs). Existing methods enhance recommendations through explicit Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning under a Think-then-Answer paradigm. However, explicit CoT incurs substantial inference overhead by generating lengthy reasoning traces and relies on manually designed templates that struggle to capture diverse, dynamic user interests. We propose OneLatent, an efficient latent reasoning framework that compresses explicit reasoning traces into several learnable latent tokens, enabling Latent-Reason-then-Answer inference without generating verbose traces. OneLatent first introduces Multi-View Adaptive CoT (MV-ACoT), which creates diverse, high-quality teacher-generated supervision by exploring user interests from multiple perspectives and automatically adapting reasoning complexity to each instance. Building on pretrained FRMs, it then uses a three-stage latent-token alignment paradigm to progressively internalize CoT traces into learnable latent tokens. Finally, a multistage curriculum-based post-training strategy activates latent-token reasoning for downstream recommendation tasks. Experiments on an industrial-scale Kuaishou dataset and the public Kuaishou LLM-Rec benchmark show that OneLatent consistently outperforms explicit CoT-based methods and traditional baselines. Compared with the Think and No-Think variants of FRMs, OneLatent improves SID@64 by 17.44% and 9.33%, respectively, while achieving over 17x higher online inference throughput. We further develop a production serving system for scalable, real-time FRM inference. An online A/B test in Kuaishou's local-services advertising scenario shows that deploying OneLatent with this system yields an estimated 9.6% revenue lift over strong online baselines, including OneRec and OneReason.

贝塞尔曲线引导的自适应流用于跨域序列推荐

8/10
Bezier-Guided Adaptive Flow for Cross-domain Sequential Recommendation
Ziang Lu, Yu Zhang, Xinzhe Jiang, Lei Sang, Yiwen Zhang
核心总结:

针对跨域序列推荐中用户偏好一致性差异大、全局对齐引入噪声的问题，提出BGAFlow框架：先用LLM推断用户画像进行双域统一预训练构建共享语义空间，再基于潜在兴趣重叠度为每个用户估计个性化迁移范围，并构造条件Bezier曲线作为几何参考、学习残差流以根据中间偏好状态逐步精炼迁移轨迹，实现状态自适应的个性化语义迁移。

个性化推荐理由:

该文聚焦跨域序列推荐中的个性化迁移控制，属于RecSys核心问题，且利用LLM推断用户画像并引入Bezier曲线流模型进行语义自适应演化，与关注的自适应Transformer/流模型及LLM在推荐中的应用方向高度契合。

2026-10-22 06:53:57 | arXiv:2610.30825v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Cross-domain sequential recommendation aims to alleviate data sparsity and cold-start problems by transferring useful preference knowledge across domains. However, users exhibit varying degrees of preference consistency across domains, while existing methods often rely on global alignment objectives or coarse-grained transfer strategies. Such approaches may introduce irrelevant information and provide limited flexibility in adapting semantic representations to individual user preferences. To address these challenges, we propose BGAFlow, a personalized framework that combines state-adaptive semantic evolution with user-specific transfer control. BGAFlow first establishes a shared semantic space through unified dual-domain pretraining with user profiles inferred by a large language model (LLM). Based on latent interest overlap, it then estimates a personalized horizon to determine the appropriate transfer extent for each user. To enable semantic adaptation, BGAFlow constructs a conditional Bezier curve as a geometric reference and learns a residual flow that progressively refines the trajectory based on intermediate preference states. The learned flow incorporates dynamic preference feedback to adjust transfer directions, while recommendation supervision and trajectory regularization encourage effective and smooth adaptation. Extensive experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate that BGAFlow consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, validating the benefits of personalized transfer control and state-adaptive semantic evolution.

面向检索增强大语言模型中搜索状态感知的自我评估强化学习

8/10
Self-Assessment Reinforcement Learning for Search-State Awareness in Retrieval-Augmented Large Language Models
Taewhoo Lee, Minju Song, Jungwoo Park, Chanwoong Yoon, Junha Jung, Hyeon Hwang, ...
核心总结:

问题：现有搜索代理缺乏对已获取与缺失信息的搜索状态感知，导致幻觉推理和过早终止。核心思想：提出自评估强化学习框架，通过双奖励设计（评估纠正效果与惩罚过度依赖）让模型在训练中学会识别自身搜索状态并优化搜索行为，无需测试时额外评估。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究检索增强LLM搜索代理的搜索状态感知能力，属于LLM在搜索/推荐领域的直接应用，其强化学习训练策略对构建更智能的搜索代理有重要参考价值。

2026-10-21 20:46:24 | arXiv:2610.30435v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Recent advances in search agents have enabled large language models to tackle complex tasks by retrieving and synthesizing external information. However, existing search agents often lack search-state awareness, i.e., the ability to recognize what information has been obtained and what remains missing, leading to hallucinated reasoning and prematurely terminated search. To address this limitation, we propose Self-Assessment Reinforcement Learning (SARL), a reinforcement learning framework in which models learn to recognize their own search state during training and improve their search behavior accordingly, without requiring additional assessment at test time. SARL achieves this through a dual reward design that captures the corrective effect of each assessment while penalizing excessive reliance on it, jointly encouraging effective and efficient search trajectories. Under this setup, SARL achieves a 24.3% relative improvement over the strongest baseline, with average gains of 35.9% on multi-hop reasoning and 27.9% on deep research benchmarks against the strongest per-benchmark baselines. SARL further surpasses methods built on auxiliary supervision or multi-agent coordination, despite operating as a standalone RL framework with no inference-time scaffolding. Beyond final performance, SARL maintains training stability under extended search horizons, a regime where existing methods consistently degrade, enabling sustained gains as task complexity grows.

跨预训练模型的LoRA叠加

8/10
LoRA Superposition across Pretrained Models
Keigo Watanabe, Hiroshi Kera, Kazuhiko Kawamoto
核心总结:

问题：探究在不同预训练骨干上独立训练的同一任务LoRA能否直接求和并应用于所有骨干。核心思路：提出LoRA叠加构造，实验发现各骨干专用LoRA在参数空间近乎正交、跨骨干响应集中于对角线，支持“冻结骨干作为隐式选择器、组件互不干扰”的假设，无需显式路由或对齐。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究LoRA在多预训练骨干上的叠加与组件非干扰机制，属于参数高效微调与Transformer架构的基础进展，对推荐/搜索系统中的多模型统一适配有借鉴价值。

2026-10-22 04:03:04 | arXiv:2610.30691v1 |
cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Low-rank adaptation (LoRA) is usually tied to the pretrained backbone on which it is learned. We ask whether LoRAs trained independently for the same task on different pretrained backbones can be summed and applied unchanged to all contributing backbones. We call this construction LoRA superposition. Across seven ViT-B/16 CLIP backbones and eight image-classification tasks, the exact sum improves all 56 backbone-task pairs and achieves a mean accuracy gain of 30.21 percentage points over the unadapted backbone. The exact sum achieves a mean accuracy only 0.28 percentage points below that of individually trained solo LoRAs. In contrast, a leave-one-model-out (LOMO) sum produces a mean gain of only 0.06 percentage points over the unadapted backbones. Backbone-specific solo LoRAs are nearly orthogonal in parameter space, while pairwise cross-backbone responses are strongly concentrated on the diagonal. These results support a component-wise non-interference hypothesis, under which the frozen backbone acts as an implicit selector of its own component without explicit routing or cross-model alignment.

Lychee：面向高召回向量搜索的倒排列表认证筛选

7/10
Lychee: Certified Screening of Inverted Lists for High-Recall Vector Search
Dongfang Zhao
核心总结:

论文解决高召回向量检索中倒排索引需全精度读取大量候选向量导致吞吐下降的问题，核心思路是利用同一列表锚点计算认证下界，在张量核上对探测向量进行认证排除，仅对少数幸存者计算精确距离，从而在理论上保证返回探测列表的精确k近邻。

个性化推荐理由:

针对向量检索高召回场景提出认证筛选机制，属于搜索/推荐基础设施的核心检索算法进步，虽不涉及LLM但与检索系统底层效率直接相关。

2026-10-22 04:10:13 | arXiv:2610.30697v1 |
cs.IR
查看完整摘要
Nearest-neighbor search over embedding vectors matches faces against watchlists, transactions against known fraud patterns, and medical images against confirmed cases, where a missed neighbor is a missed threat, fraud, or diagnosis. Because an approximate index cannot report which queries lost a true neighbor, these applications require recall of 0.999 to 0.9999, at a cost far below that of exact search over billions of vectors. Inverted-file indexes reach high recall by probing more lists, but they read and score every probed vector at full precision, so that throughput falls in inverse proportion to the number of probed lists. Graph indexes saturate below these targets, whereas quantized lists must rerank their candidates with the full vectors. Our insight is to decide for almost every probed vector that it cannot enter the top k of the probed lists without reading the vector, through the certified lower bound of exact search by certified exclusion. This paper turns this insight into Lychee, a GPU inverted-file search that screens the probed lists on tensor cores under a threshold from anchors of the same lists and computes exact distances only for the few survivors. We prove that Lychee returns exactly the k nearest vectors of the probed lists, the answer of an inverted-file search at full precision, while it reads a few hundred full vectors per query regardless of the number of probed lists. On eleven datasets from one million to one billion vectors and against ten GPU and CPU baselines, Lychee is 9.7 to 117 times faster than IVF-Flat at equal recall with 200 probed lists on datasets of up to 100 million vectors. It also reaches recall levels that CAGRA and HNSW do not reach and outruns exact search by certified exclusion by 5 to 18 times above ten million vectors. On four GPUs, Lychee searches one billion vectors at recall 0.9999, 44.5 times faster than IVF-Flat on BIGANN-1B.

Delta N-gram：向语言模型原生N-gram记忆中进行仅前向的持续写入

7/10
Delta N-gram: Forward-Only Continual Writing into a Language Model's Native N-gram Memory
Hao Wu
核心总结:

要解决的问题是语言模型如何在不修改预训练参数、不依赖反向传播的前提下持续写入新知识；核心方法是把原生哈希N-gram表T^0冻结，用4.1M参数writer对每个(position,hash head)事件生成门控目标，并通过块平均delta规则写入稀疏补丁Δ，使每行保持旧值与门控目标的凸组合，使用仅前向、可清除还原。

个性化推荐理由:

提出前向-only的N-gram记忆增量写入机制，属于核心模型持续学习/记忆编辑技术，对RecSys/搜索中用户序列快速适配和知识更新有直接启发，但未涉及推荐搜索具体任务。

2026-10-22 11:42:49 | arXiv:2610.31125v1 |
cs.CLcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Our previous analysis found that a model's native hashed N-gram table is where continual updates disturb the base least, but its writes needed backpropagation and changed the pretrained table. We present Delta N-gram, a forward-only write path into that table. The pretrained table T^0 is never modified. Evidence is written into a sparse patch Δon the rows it addresses: a 4.1M-parameter writer emits a target and a gate per (position, hash head) event, and a block-averaged delta rule keeps each row a convex combination of its old value and the gated targets. The writer is trained offline on raw text with the pretraining objective; at use time writing takes forward passes only, and clearing Δrestores the original model. A 0.6B prototype trained to answer questions keeps 16/16 facts after a second write; online gradient writes keep 2--4/16. We study two writers: a plain writer that encodes evidence with the T^0-only model, and a delta writer that reads its current memory while encoding. On Qwen3.8-Flash-Next, the plain writer's memory is document-specific: on 64 held-out news events it lowers continuation NLL by 0.068 nats/token against empty memory and by 0.024 against another document's memory, and 0.063 and 0.013 after 16 intervening articles. The delta writer's memory is more document-specific and keeps more of it under distraction (70% vs 54%). Over 35,000 writes on our previous seven-task chain both writers stay stable and the delta writer drifts less (layer-2 math drift 176% vs 188%); later accuracy changes by -0.26/-0.52pp (-7.29pp for gradient updates) and small-k_i drift is 17.9/17.5% (39.2%). Acquisition is negative, and general-input drift reaches 32.8% (20.75%): format rows read by every example grow to 4--12 times their pretrained norm. Zeroing eight such rows removes over 98% of layer-2 drift on math inputs, together with their NLL gain.

ULME：面向嵌入与语言建模相互增强的预测耦合方法

7/10
ULME: Predictive Coupling for Mutually Reinforcing Embedding and Language Modeling
Yang Liu, Zhiqiang Yuan, Shu-Dong Huang, Jiancheng Lv, Liwei Yang, Qiwei Yan, Ka...
核心总结:

问题：现有统一嵌入-生成模型只是共享参数但两种能力互不塑造。核心思想：在后训练中于query与answer间插入可学习slot链，分别读取语义嵌入与推理嵌入，用对比损失、语言建模损失和停止梯度预测损失在共享因果主干上联合训练，使生成反向塑造嵌入编码，实现能力互相增强。

个性化推荐理由:

提出嵌入与语言建模在因果解码器上通过预测耦合互相增强，属于LLM核心能力统一的技术进展，但对RecSys/Search/Ads仅是间接赋能（更好的检索嵌入），未直接涉及推荐或广告系统。

2026-10-22 04:23:07 | arXiv:2610.30705v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Text embedding and text generation are two of the most useful capabilities of modern large language models, yet they are typically provided by separate specialized models (an embedder and a generator) trained with incompatible objectives and deployed side by side in retrieval-augmented generation and agent systems. Recent unified embedding-generation models such as GritLM share parameters between the two capabilities, but they either switch attention regimes between modes or optimize embedding and language modeling as parallel objectives that never supervise each other. We argue that these designs are only partially unified: the two capabilities coexist on the same weights, but do not shape each other. We propose ULME, a post-training framework that unifies embedding and language modeling on a causal decoder-only LLM through predictive coupling. ULME inserts a chain of learnable slots between the query and the answer, reads a semantic embedding and a reasoning embedding from two designated slots, and trains three objectives on a shared causal backbone: a contrastive loss anchoring the semantic slot to document representations, a language-modeling loss supervising answer generation, and a stop-gradient predictive loss that aligns the reasoning slot with a pooled summary of the answer, so that generation actively shapes what the embedding encodes. Across two model families (Qwen2.5, Qwen3) and eight scales spanning 0.5B-8B parameters, a single ULME checkpoint improves MTEB by 3.3-4.1 points over the strongest open-source baseline, tightens BRIGHT and AdvBench-IR simultaneously, and remains on par with a generation-only fine-tune on standard accuracy benchmarks (MMLU, GSM8K, MMLU-Pro, HumanEval), demonstrating that genuine unification yields mutually reinforcing rather than merely coexisting capabilities.

融合税：混合检索何时造成损害的诊断分析

6/10
The Fusion Tax: A Diagnostic Analysis of When Hybrid Retrieval Hurts
Aaditya Chauhan, Arun Agarwal
核心总结:

问题：混合检索融合稀疏与稠密检索器时，聚合增益掩盖了单查询上的性能损失。核心思想：将“不融合”视为一等决策，提出融合税概念，发现最优融合方法仍在13-48%查询上劣于更优组件；融合税是保持召回率的排序效应，在仅一个检索器覆盖的查询上最可能发生，且RRF在稠密强时税更高；融合决策应依据目标语料的稀疏-稠密平衡度量。

个性化推荐理由:

研究混合检索中融合对单查询的负面影响（融合税），直接关联搜索/推荐系统的稀疏-稠密融合策略，但未涉及LLM或Transformer架构创新。

2026-10-22 02:24:08 | arXiv:2610.30612v1 |
cs.IRcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Hybrid retrieval, which fuses a sparse lexical retriever with a dense neural one, is widely recommended in the software documentation surveyed in September 2026, usually with reciprocal rank fusion (RRF), on the strength of aggregate gains. We ask what fusion costs on individual queries, treating "do not fuse" as a first-class outcome, in the setting in which hybrid retrieval is deployed: a standard Anserini BM25, five dense encoders spanning 22M-596M parameters and three model generations, a learned-sparse (SPLADE) alternative, and a cross-encoder second stage, on seven BEIR datasets and three fusion functions. Even the best method, dense-weighted score interpolation, ranks relevant documents below the better of its two components on 13-48% of queries and below the deployable dense-only retriever on 8-21% (14-48% and 11-25% at a weight selected under leave-one-corpus-out validation), while beating dense-only in aggregate on every corpus with the primary encoder; the downside is significant under loss-averse risk-sensitive evaluation on six of the seven corpora. We call this loss the fusion tax. It is mostly a ranking effect that preserves recall in aggregate, it is most likely on the queries that only one retriever covers, and it persists across the encoder ladder and the SPLADE substitution. RRF carries the higher tax wherever the dense retriever is clearly stronger and only beats dense-only where the two components are balanced. Fusion failure is weakly predictable within a corpus but transfers unreliably across corpora, because the features that predict it change sign with the sparse-dense balance. A cross-encoder recovers part of the tax only where it is itself reliable. We distill these findings into guidance that keys the fusion decision to the measured sparse-dense balance of the target corpus.

多Token预测与替代分词提升大规模隐式多跳图搜索学习

6/10
Multi-Token Prediction and Alternative Tokenization Improve Learning Latent Multi-Hop Graph Search at Scale
Arvid Frydenlund, FuTe Wong, Katherine Lambert, Frank Rudzicz
核心总结:

问题：LLM在大规模图上学习多跳最短路径搜索随规模增大而失效；核心发现：失效源于监督信号（单token预测）和分词方式两个正交原因，多token预测（MTP）可有效且拓扑无关地改善该任务，替代分词方案与MTP互补，从而突破此前的规模扩展墙。

个性化推荐理由:

论文研究LLM学习图上多跳最短路径搜索，属于核心LLM技术在搜索/推荐图结构数据上的基础能力探索，但与实际RecSys/Search/Ads系统的直接关联较弱，且未涉及Transformer架构或VLM相关创新。

2026-10-22 15:35:50 | arXiv:2610.31439v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Given an unseen graph, a start node, $s$, and an end node, $t$, can a language model generate the shortest path from $s$ to $t$? Prior work has found that models fail to learn this task as the graphs scale. We show that this failure has two causes: the supervision signal and the tokenization. First, we theoretically establish and empirically demonstrate the effectiveness of multi-token prediction (MTP), and show that the benefits of MTP are topologically agnostic. Second, we introduce alternative tokenizations and show that the two causes are orthogonal. Using these methods, we break through the scaling wall observed by prior work, consistently and stably learning to solve the task at scales far beyond previous limits.

SQuAT：球面格向量量化感知训练

6/10
SQuAT: Spherical Lattice Vector Quantization-Aware Training
Sebastian Loeschcke, Ivan Koryakovskiy, Tycho F. A. van der Ouderaa, Riccardo De...
核心总结:

研究问题：如何将格点向量量化（VQ）可扩展地用于十亿参数LLM的量化感知训练，并理解其在QAT下的行为。核心思想：SQuAT将权重分解为方向与幅度，方向用E_8派生球面码本、幅度用独立低比特标量量化器，并分别施加独立STE，使方向更新落入切空间而隐式形成球面（黎曼）优化；同时用分布式精确最近邻分配（仅通信离散索引）实现大规模训练加速。

个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦球面格点向量量化的量化感知训练（VQ-QAT），属于LLM低比特压缩与效率技术，对推荐/搜索/广告的LLM部署有间接支撑价值，但不直接针对RecSys/Search/Ads建模创新。

2026-10-22 15:14:44 | arXiv:2610.31412v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Vector quantization (VQ), in particular lattice-based methods, offer strong rate-distortion trade-offs for low-bit large language models (LLMs), yet their behavior under quantization-aware training (VQ-QAT) remains poorly understood. We scale lattice VQ-QAT to billion-parameter LLMs and multi-billion-token training regimes, and introduce SQuAT, a shape-gain VQ-QAT approach trained with a separate straight-through estimator (STE) for the shape and gain. SQuAT decomposes weights into direction and magnitude: normalized directions are quantized using an $E_8$-derived spherical codebook, while gains are quantized independently with low-bit scalar quantizers. We show that, unlike a standard STE applied to the full quantizer, which ignores the shape-gain structure, these separate STEs induce tangent-space updates for directions, yielding an implicit form of spherical (Riemannian) optimization. To enable scalability, we develop a distributed exact nearest-neighbor assignment scheme, in which workers quantize disjoint weight blocks and communicate only discrete indices, reducing VQ-QAT training time by 12.8$\times$ and allowing us to scale it to 10B tokens. Across instruction-tuned LLaMA-3.2-1B/3B, Qwen3-4B, and base Qwen3-1.7B, SQuAT consistently outperforms standard VQ-QAT STE under identical quantizers and initialization, and achieves SOTA VQ-QAT accuracy.

cs.CVcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Fine-grained image-text retrieval requires a model not only to assess global semantic consistency between an image and a text, but also to model visual evidence for local textual semantics, including objects, attributes, quantities, actions, and relations, so as to distinguish semantically similar candidates. Existing methods commonly use fixed image patches, detected regions, or text tokens as the basic matching units and then aggregate local interactions through max pooling, cross attention, or similarity accumulation. Such strategies remain limited in semantic unit construction and global matching consistency. This paper proposes a hierarchical fine-grained cross-modal alignment framework, termed HFCMA. The framework progressively transforms low-level patch and token representations into adaptive visual regions and textual phrases, and then performs structured region-phrase matching within a global retrieval scoring process. Specifically, HFCMA first adopts dynamic granularity discovery (DGD) to aggregate raw patches and tokens into semantically compact visual regions and textual phrases. It then formulates region-phrase matching as an entropy-regularized optimal transport problem, thereby estimating a structured alignment matrix under global marginal constraints. To reduce background noise and local semantic drift, HFCMA further introduces background debiasing, textual importance weighting, and prototype consistency regularization. Experiments on Flickr30K and MS-COCO show that HFCMA effectively combines dynamic semantic unit construction with OT-based structured matching under a dual-encoder retrieval framework. The resulting fine-grained score provides structured local alignment information for candidate ranking and improves the discrimination of subtle semantic differences.

2026-10-22 17:56:13 | arXiv:2610.31646v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Many recent open-weight language models use n-gram embedding tables to improve performance. These tables store learned representations of short token sequences, such as pairs or triples of consecutive tokens, which the model retrieves during processing. However, they can require substantial memory: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, for example, allocates 196 billion parameters to its Engram n-gram memory. We therefore investigate whether this memory cost can be reduced while preserving the performance improvement that the tables provide. We propose storing n-gram embeddings at low precision and systematically investigate different quantization methods and bit allocations across tables. Across nanochat-, Llama-, and GPT-2-family models trained from scratch, four-bit tables save approximately 75% of table memory while preserving more than 99% of the validation-loss improvement from adding the tables. Using fewer bits leads to a larger drop in performance, although we find that a short fine-tuning phase recovers a significant part of this loss. In addition, reinvesting the saved memory in larger tables can improve performance beyond that of the original full-precision tables while still using less memory. These table memory savings also extend to large existing models: on DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash and Qwen3.8-Flash-Next, low-bit tables save 47-55% of table memory, with perplexity increases of less than 1.2% and largely preserved downstream task performance. These findings establish low-bit precision as a practical way to reduce the memory requirements of n-gram embeddings and use the saved capacity to improve model performance.

查看完整摘要
Users increasingly rely on AI systems for extended, multi-turn tasks, yet evidence indicates that LLM-based system performance degrades over prolonged interaction. To evaluate and improve AI assistants in such settings, we need user simulators that faithfully follow complex user intents while exhibiting realistic conversational behavior. Existing approaches manifest a trade-off: prompted assistants can follow complex user intents but lack behavioral fidelity, while specialized user simulators better approximate conversational behavior but struggle to remain faithful to complex intents. In this paper, we propose a reinforcement learning (RL) recipe that jointly rewards task intent adherence and behavioral naturalness. A crucial contribution of the method is operationalizing naturalness with a lightweight discriminator trained in an alternating, adversarial loop with the user simulator. cs.CV
查看完整摘要
While the Platonic Representation Hypothesis posits that vision and language models converge toward compatible latent geometries, recovering class-level correspondences between unpaired modalities without cross-modal supervision remains challenging. Existing relational matching methods compare within-modality similarity kernels directly, although the same semantic structure may be represented through different numerical parameterizations across modalities. We propose RankAlign, a rank-based normalization strategy for class-level unpaired vision-language matching. RankAlign transforms each within-modality similarity kernel into an ordinal relational representation before permutation matching, while leaving the correspondence space and matching solver unchanged. The resulting representation is invariant to strictly increasing reparameterizations of similarity values while preserving the ordinal structure used for matching. We provide exact-recovery and stability guarantees that characterize correspondence recovery under both exact and partial ordinal agreement. Experiments across diverse frozen vision and language encoders and multiple datasets demonstrate improved and more stable class-level correspondence recovery, including under label corruption and domain shift.

cs.AIcs.IR
查看完整摘要
Scaling large language models has substantially improved linguistic fluency, yet reliable autonomous agents require more than fluent generation. In multi-turn inference, current agentic systems often produce semantically plausible but structurally ungrounded reasoning, especially under incomplete observations. We propose Structure-Grounded Uncertainty Reduction (SGUR), a training-free framework that complements token-level generation with active uncertainty reduction over structured hypothesis spaces. SGUR represents incomplete observations as a query graph and aligns them with candidate hypothesis graphs through a prior-weighted partial graph matching mechanism, where database-derived empirical priors assign greater importance to decision-relevant relations rather than treating all graph edges uniformly. The resulting inference state is iteratively refined by an active information-gain strategy that selects the next most informative node to query, systematically pruning the candidate space. We instantiate SGUR on a challenging PrimeKG-derived sequential medical diagnosis benchmark, demonstrating that structural grounding can partially substitute for model scale: starting from only two observed symptoms, SGUR achieves 92.5% Hit@3, outperforming the strongest Graph-RAG baseline by 9.4 percentage points while using approximately 5x fewer tokens. Notably, SGUR enables smaller backbone models to match or exceed the performance of models up to 10x larger, suggesting that structural grounding provides a more robust and efficient paradigm for scaling agent performance than relying exclusively on increasing model size.

cs.CLcs.AIcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Looped and depth-grown language models reuse computations across depth and have attracted interest for their reasoning capabilities. Building on prior suggestive evidence of improved in-context-learning (ICL), we investigate how such reuse favors ICL, including how it shapes the acquisition, retention, and mechanisms of this ability. Our analysis spans pretrained language models up to 3B parameters and controlled tasks. The experiments provide evidence for a shared inductive bias toward learning from context: grown and looped models rely more on information in context and, compared with a full-depth dense reference, acquire ICL earlier in controlled tasks and retain it more strongly when learning fixed associations in the weights is also possible. Tracing attention heads through training reveals that depth growth propagates early induction heads across layers. Ablations show that grown and looped models rely more strongly on induction-ranked heads for both few-shot performance and later-token prediction. Together, these findings provide evidence for a shared inductive bias toward learning from context and suggest that (soft) parameter reuse shapes how this ability develops and which attention mechanisms support it.

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Training-free personalization adapts a frozen vision-language model (VLM) to recognize user-defined concepts from only a few exemplars by retrieving concept entries at inference time. While recent retriever-based methods enrich each concept with descriptive evidence, they largely treat concepts in isolation, so the retrieved information can be plausible yet non-discriminative, especially when the personalized concept set contains highly confusable, fine-grained categories. We argue that personalization is inherently relational: correct recognition depends on the specific distinctions between a concept and its nearest alternatives. To this end, we propose ReCoG, a Relational Concept Graph for retrieval-augmented personalization. ReCoG represents concepts as nodes and augments them with directed edges that explicitly capture discriminative cues between concept pairs, elicited by a frozen VLM during database construction. At inference, ReCoG retrieves a shortlist and resolves ambiguity via explicit pairwise comparisons conditioned on the corresponding relational edges, selecting the concept that consistently wins against competing candidates. We also introduce FINGER benchmark to test personalized recognition on confusable concept sets. Across FINGER, ReCoG substantially outperforms prior methods, with the largest gains in the most confusable regimes. ReCoG also achieves consistently best performance on established personalization benchmarks including captioning and VQA, confirming that relational evidence is broadly beneficial beyond fine-grained settings. Project Page: https://csy-98.github.io/ReCoG/

查看完整摘要
Deep neural networks often struggle to provide reliable confidence estimates, making post-hoc calibration essential for reliable decision-making. Existing post-hoc methods largely rely on score-based transformations, which improve marginal calibration but often fail to capture spatially varying miscalibration in local regions of the learned feature space. To address this gap, we propose Local Adaptive Probability Estimation (LAPE), a post-hoc residual calibration framework. Specifically, LAPE treats the global calibrator output as a stable anchor and refines the individual prediction by leveraging non-parametric local evidence from neighboring calibration samples. As a plug-and-play refinement module, LAPE effectively utilizes the information about feature-space geometry and can reuse the calibration data that are used to construct the global calibrator. Furthermore, we extend LAPE to the setting with multi-view inputs through Cascade Feature Augmentation Fusion (CFAF). Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed methods can improve the calibration quality of various existing baselines and remain efficient and robust across different settings.

Chunking strategies for retrieval-augmented generation are almost always evaluated on a static snapshot, yet the documents underlying a knowledge base continue to change. Because chunks are embedded independently, a boundary that moves after an edit forces unchanged text to be re-embedded. We measure this update cost on 120 pairs of adjacent Chinese and English Wikipedia revisions for three stateless chunkers (non-overlapping fixed-size, recursive paragraph-first, and content-defined chunking) under a strict byte-identical reuse rule and a frozen dense encoder. On the 116-article common-success pool, with frozen, length-calibrated parameters, fixed-size chunking recomputes 57-68% of its full new-version embedding-input workload, recursive chunking 38-56%, and content-defined chunking 43-54% (it failed on 4 of 62 Chinese articles), while at these parameters mean evidence recall under a fixed 2048-token retrieval budget differs by less than two percentage points within either language. A cost-only parameter grid shows that the ranking of the two content-aware chunkers changes with chunk-length settings, whereas non-overlapping fixed-size chunking has the highest recomputation cost in every length-matched condition. Controlled edits and a verbatim-presence diagnostic support boundary drift as one contributor to recomputation of text already present in the previous version. Update cost is therefore a substantial, parameter-sensitive dimension that static retrieval evaluations do not expose and that should be reported alongside retrieval quality.

Speculative decoding is a popular decode acceleration method. As such, it is particularly relevant to long-output generation, which requires an increasingly large KV cache. KV cache compression offers a way to constrain costs in long-context drafting. However, compression methods that focus on prefill information can lose relevance as the output length grows. We term this phenomenon decode staleness and show that it degrades draft model accuracy under selection-based KV cache compression. We introduce RIFT, a family of selection-based KV cache compression strategies that balance prefill information and recent generated tokens. We show that refreshing the KV cache selection during token generation significantly improves the drafter's quality at the cost of additional decode latency. We further demonstrate how a compressibility-driven drafter training phase, based on sparse attention masks, can improve the drafter's quality under a compressed KV cache. Many language-model pipelines end in a closed-set decision, such as selecting a tool. Prompting a large language model for it is slow and order-sensitive. We ask how small an encoder for such decisions can be and whether it should read the options jointly or separately. In a three-seed study at 32 million parameters, per-option scoring beats joint scoring on a held-out sample of a 37-benchmark decision suite by 5.8 points of chance-corrected skill at matched optimizer steps and by 2.8 at approximately matched training compute; two external benchmarks agree, though not significantly on their unseen tasks. Per-option scoring needs 5 to 13 times more encoder passes: at most twice the median latency, but more than four times the mean. The smallest answers a median request in 14 milliseconds on 16 processor threads, against 728 for the classifier. cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Domain generalization (DG) aims to improve the generalization of the model to any unseen domains. Targeting this goal, existing DG methods mainly focus on learning domain-invariant features or prompts from the given source domains. However, these methods rely on source-domain data, and collecting and annotating large-scale single- or multi-source datasets is often infeasible, limiting the practical applicability of DG. To tackle this challenge, we propose {\underline{Do}}main \underline{P}rompt Learning (DoP), a method built on a large vision-language model (e.g., CLIP) for zero-shot domain generalization, which recognizes unseen classes in unseen target domains without access to source-domain data. DoP essentially learns the target domain knowledge via prompt both in text and visual modalities to overcome the domain shift in text-visual modalities under the test-time adaptation formulation. Specifically, DoP first takes text domain learning (TDL) to simulate various distribution shifts by learning diverse style tokens implicitly in the text space with inter-style diversity loss and intra-style consistency loss. Subsequently, DoP employs visual domain learning (VDL) to further refine the target-domain-relevant style token generated by TDL by making use of the test visual data with self-supervised learning. Extensive experiments on popular DG benchmark datasets (e.g., PACS, VLCS, OfficeHome, and DomainNet) and ImageNet show that our DoP consistently outperforms the state-of-the-art DG approaches. Codes are available at: https://github.com/shiming-chen/DoP.

cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
LLMs have shown strong in-context learning (ICL) capability, but extending it to Multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) remains challenging. Prior multimodal ICL methods often rely on ICL-specific datasets for additional training or task vector extraction, which can improve performance on the same ICL benchmark used for adaptation. However, they do not generalize well to other benchmarks and often induce forgetting in previously well-solved tasks. For this reason, we identify 'visual grounding' as the key bottleneck in multimodal ICL; MLLMs often fail to attend to task-relevant visual evidence in demonstrations, instead over-relying on textual cues and producing hallucinated outputs. To address this, we propose ProCoRe to enhance visual grounding through contrastive reinforcement learning on multimodal contrastive data for improving multimodal ICL. ProCoRe generates captions for contrastive image pairs, decomposes them into propositions, and optimizes contrastive rewards that encourage alignment with corresponding images while discouraging mismatched ones. Despite never seeing ICL-formatted training examples, ProCoRe improves average multimodal ICL classification accuracy by 5.6% and captioning ROUGE-L by 13.8% over the off-the-shelf Qwen-VL-3-8B model, outperforming ICL-data-dependent state of the arts across benchmarks. Finally, we introduce PICL, a personalized multimodal ICL captioning benchmark that tests whether MLLMs genuinely understand visual demonstrations rather than relying on pre-trained semantic priors.

Compiling Autoregressive LLM Control into Policy-Exact Parallel Inference for Hierarchical RAG
Zehao Lin, Qianfeng Shen, Xixuan Hao, Shaobo Cui, Chunyu Li, Kai Chen, Bo Tang, ...

2026-10-22 03:25:21 | arXiv:2610.30664v1 |
cs.CLcs.IR
查看完整摘要
Large language models increasingly act as controllers in structured decision pipelines, yet their decisions are still executed by autoregressive text decoding. Hierarchical RAG is one such setting: at each frontier of a document tree, an LLM commits to $b$ of $k$ sections under an input-dependent action grammar, executed as either failure-prone free-form JSON or reliable but strictly serial grammar-constrained generation. We show that this serialization is unnecessary for bounded-horizon control: the reachable future forms a shallow action-prefix tree, which we compile --- one root prefill plus one tree-causal probe evaluate every prefix-conditioned action distribution with serial decoding's tokens, positions, and causal ancestry. A prefix-state lemma makes compiled control policy-exact in exact arithmetic: the same policy with a two-forward critical path and zero output tokens. One further probe pass certifies the exact order-marginalized MAP set. Across four document-disjoint splits and two scales on the primary MetaX device, compiled control matches the serial policy on $84$--$89\%$ of questions --- every instrumented divergence a floating-point near-tie --- and cuts control latency by $35$--$47\%$ versus token-wise serial decoding and $6$--$7\%$ versus an idealized chunked engine; an H800 repetition preserves the ordering.

HERD：用于持续学习的增强子演化递归蒸馏

3/10
HERD: Harness-Evolving Recursive Distillation for Continual Learning
Xun Sun, Thanh Thi Nguyen, Thuy-Trang Vu
个性化推荐理由:

该论文提出了一种用于持续学习的递归知识蒸馏框架，并采用增强子演化机制。持续学习与推荐系统、搜索和广告场景中的模型漂移与灾难性遗忘问题高度相关。然而，该工作似乎是一个通用的持续学习技术，而非专门针对推荐系统的技术，因此其直接相关性有限。

2026-10-22 15:03:20 | arXiv:2610.31401v1 |
cs.CLcs.AIcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Continual learning requires a model to keep acquiring new skills and knowledge without compromising its existing capabilities. Parametric methods suffer from catastrophic forgetting and loss of plasticity. Non-parametric methods, such as prompts, skills, and harnesses, leverage in-context learning but are limited by context length and lossy plain-text compression. To effectively combine their complementary strengths, we propose HERD (Harness-Evolving Recursive Distillation), a hybrid continual learning framework that learns efficiently in the harness and internalizes experience into cumulative capabilities via distillation. On the harness side, HERD continually refines its skills to capture reusable patterns, while retaining high-residual examples that the skills fail to capture in a bounded memory. On the parameter side, HERD performs drift-constrained distillation with dual anchors, substantially reducing forgetting over long recursive runs. On the 25-stage task stream of TRACE+17, under fixed memory and compute budgets, HERD matches the strongest parametric baseline in new-task acquisition while exhibiting the least forgetting and the strongest retention of general capabilities.

2026-10-22 14:08:48 | arXiv:2610.31314v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Large language models can repeat an instruction while failing to keep obeying it. We study this recall-compliance dissociation in long-horizon dialogue and define State Integrity Collapse: a model preserves a stated constraint in memory but stops applying it to later turns. We introduce StateTrack-Bench, a controlled suite of conversations covering numerical, format, role/persona, factual-memory, and logical-rule constraints. Across seven provider-pinned model snapshots and five dialogue-depth probes, strict joint retention ranges from 62.4% to 100.0%, with degradation concentrated in symbolic constraints and in one especially brittle model. A forked recall audit shows that 88.6% of observed failures are behavioral-drift failures rather than ordinary forgetting; excluding the brittle outlier preserves this diagnosis (84.4% of failures). Extended evaluations at depths up to 50 turns confirm that the dissociation persists beyond the core benchmark, while Structured State Anchoring recovers up to 24.4 points on immutable constraints. The results show that reliability cannot be inferred from single-turn instruction following alone; evaluation must track whether remembered constraints remain operational as dialogue state grows. Code and artifacts are available at: https://github.com/xzzvsxd/StateTrack.

2026-10-22 14:04:07 | arXiv:2610.31305v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Hierarchical language generation separates continuous semantic modeling from discrete text realization, but its effectiveness depends on what the latent space compresses and how the discrete generator uses it. We propose Cola2, a framework that jointly studies continuous semantic compression, the balance between reconstruction and generation, and continuous-discrete co-adaptation. Cola2 dynamically segments text into semantic spans, controls the compression rate through auxiliary-loss-free negative feedback, and combines reconstruction and generation supervision to learn compact, predictive latent representations. A block-causal masked diffusion decoder is trained with clean, noisy, and dropped latent conditions, while classifier-free guidance adjusts the balance between semantic reconstruction and autonomous generation. Token-prediction gradients update the encoder through the latents, whereas Flow Matching prior learning is decoupled from representation learning by stop-gradient. During inference, generated text is re-encoded under a fixed span layout to keep the latent history consistent with the actual text context. Experiments on five downstream tasks with about 210 billion training tokens validate the core designs and show competitive, unsaturated scaling. With 346.03 billion source tokens, an unconverged checkpoint reaches a 60.10 average across eight tasks and surpasses Llama-3-8B on HellaSwag and StoryCloze despite its reported pretraining budget being over 43 times larger. These results demonstrate the potential of continuous-latent hierarchical language generation.

2026-10-22 14:01:31 | arXiv:2610.31303v1 |
cs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Memory enables language agents to carry useful distinctions from past experience into future decisions. Its value depends on which distinctions survive, whether a bounded agent can use them, and what can be recovered or revised as demands evolve. We develop a decision-theoretic framework that follows this value from raw history through persistent state, operational use, and costly recovery and revision. Original and retained value are expressed in units of avoided task loss; risk-cost frontiers describe attainable recovery and preservation across uses. Unknown demand yields value profiles, while interactive feedback changes the distribution of future work. A cost-aware simulation condition and five minimal counterexamples expose the roles of information timing, retrieval access, component interaction, feedback and demand uncertainty. In particular, a pointer with zero mutual information about the answer can still reduce the cost of exact recovery. Diagnostics on LoCoMo, a two-project update task and longitudinal cases exhibit failures of preservation, use and measurement. At fixed retrieved evidence, a saved ledger changes subsequent answers; at fixed agent outputs, an evaluator revision changes scores. The framework connects these distinctions to operational risk bounds and testable principles for allocating memory, preserving recovery options and revising evidence dependencies.

Memory enables language model assistants to maintain continuity across sessions and generate personalized responses, but knowing more about a user does not necessarily produce responses they prefer. Existing evaluations assess memory capabilities without directly establishing whether memory use benefits the user. To study this gap, we collect approximately 1,000 pairwise judgments from 87 participants using their own ChatGPT queries and personal memories. We find that memory-conditioned responses are not consistently preferred over responses generated without memory and in some cases, users strongly prefer the latter. Automatic evaluators also show limited agreement with users and favor memory-conditioned responses substantially more often than users do. Motivated by these gaps, we introduce a memory-aware reward model that learns from user judgments, achieving 72.8\% preference prediction accuracy and outperforming the existing baselines. cs.CLcs.AIcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Language models are compared on question-answering benchmarks that pose each question in one wording, and a reworded question can change which of two models scores higher. We ask whether giving the models tools makes such comparisons more stable. An accuracy gain cannot answer this, because tools change several steps between the question and the scored answer at once. We propose a matched protocol that separates those steps: the answer readout, reasoning, the decision to call, the tool's answer, and the report supplied without a call. We apply it to six open models in five domains under five wordings. We find that tools raise accuracy where a program answers the question and widen the wording sensitivity of many pairwise comparisons. The widening follows how often a model calls, and call frequency follows the wording for some models and not others. It also depends on the interface and the instructions: a native tool-calling interface and a second tool description leave no detected widening in molecules, and sensitivity persists where tools return intermediate results. These findings show that deterministic reports do not guarantee stable comparisons. We recommend reporting call frequency and comparison stability under several wordings beside accuracy.

cs.CLcs.AIcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Long-context models often use answer-bearing evidence less effectively when it appears away from a prompt boundary. We compare this position bias across Transformer, state-space, and hybrid Mamba architectures by moving one gold document through a fixed set of nine distractors. The evaluated Transformers show a beginning advantage that the evaluated state-space models lack, while both families favor evidence near the end. A matched pure-versus-hybrid Mamba comparison points in the same direction but remains statistically uncertain. The family difference appears among models that can answer the task. Transformer primacy reappears on synthetic retrieval, where state-space ceiling prevents a family comparison. A corpus swap within one state-space architecture changes overall accuracy without a detectable change in curve shape. Prompt changes can create or nearly remove a beginning advantage. Position remains decodable across architectures even when response curves differ, and attention concentration near initial tokens covaries with beginning sensitivity in the tested Transformer family. These exploratory results show that effective context use depends on the evaluated architecture family, capability, task, and prompt, without isolating the sequence mixer as the sole cause.

查看完整摘要
Web agents increasingly serve the same user across repeated online tasks such as booking travel, shopping, and scheduling, so their long-term usefulness depends on how well they know the user's preferences. Passive memory and personalization methods cannot obtain preferences the user never reveals, and clarification methods that value a question by its benefit to the current task overlook the benefit of reusing the answer in later tasks. We study Active Preference Acquisition (APA): which missing preferences are worth the cost of asking? We define the Prospective Personalization Value (PPV) of a missing preference as its expected cumulative utility gain over future tasks minus the effort and disclosure-risk cost of asking, and select questions under a per-interaction budget. We characterize when cumulative reuse justifies acquisition despite insufficient single-task benefit, and evaluate this regime with an initial-profile diagnostic. APA computes the counterfactual gain of each candidate preference on public task prototypes with the agent's own task solver and aggregates it by the expected reuse of each task family. We evaluate it on ActiveWebPref, a controlled longitudinal benchmark with hidden but queryable user profiles and exact task utilities. At a horizon of 20 tasks, 34.4% of budget-feasible valuable missing preferences are amortization-dependent; APA acquires 80.3% of them, whereas current-task value of information acquires 5.1%. Under per-user asking-cost caps matched to APA's realized expenditure, APA achieves higher net utility than four heuristic selection baselines. In our cost setting, asking about every unknown preference yields lower net utility than not asking, while APA recovers 84.2% of its utility gain with 27.6% of its questions. The value of acquiring a user preference should account for its reuse across future tasks, not only its benefit to the task at hand.

This creates two connected challenges: using historical conditions to identify the knowledge needed for a new request, and applying that knowledge to the user's situation. Existing benchmarks advance memory recall, personalized retrieval, and memory-guided action, while the joint evaluation of these two challenges remains underexplored. We introduce ColaBench, a synthetic benchmark in health and daily living, with 349 tasks across 10 Profiles and a knowledge base of 13,033 documents. cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Large language models (LLMs) often fail to utilise the context given in the input, resulting in hallucinations or a fallback to parametric knowledge. Context augmentation methods aim to improve model performance by modifying the input, from inserting filler tokens that add computational time to adding relevant content such as elaboration of a context during inference. However, these methods have been evaluated in isolation and only by their performance gains, leaving it unclear how they compare and whether they reliably improve performance. In this paper, we present an evaluation framework spanning five methods that differ in how informative the inserted content is and apply them at the sentence level across seven models spanning multiple sizes on three datasets. We find that accuracy gains are not always genuine improvements but undirected perturbations: the methods correct some predictions while breaking others, particularly when the inserted text allows only for more computational time. Our analysis reveals that methods that bring no additional content primarily change model predictions near the decision boundary. Our findings highlight the need to evaluate the reliability of the accuracy gains before adopting them in practice.

IQuest-Q1 is developed through a three-stage process: (1) stable and efficient pre-training, combining Stable SplitNorm, Muon, and muP with a two-stage curriculum whose second stage increases the coding-data mixture, preserving a wide stability margin between safe and optimal learning rates; (2) fine-grained mid-training, expanding the data mixture across domains and formats and progressively extending the context; and (3) multi-scaffold with multi-version post-training, combining SFT, domain-specialized RL, and MOPD across various scaffolds to integrate complementary training signals and strengthen agentic coding capabilities. Furthermore, model merging combines different experts and checkpoints from different stages while mitigating conflicts among their parameter updates. The resulting IQuest-Q1 demonstrates capabilities in both coding and general tasks. We release IQuest-Q1 as an open-source model to support community research and development.

ReflectPO: Reflection-Guided Policy Optimization for Long-Horizon LLM Agents
Rumei Li, Xunliang Cai
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于长程LLM智能体的强化学习策略优化，通过反思机制提升智能体决策能力，属于RL与LLM结合的智能体方向。虽然智能体技术可间接应用于搜索或推荐中的多步决策（如多轮检索、会话推荐），但本文未涉及RecSys/Search/Ads场景，且RL方法在推荐/搜索中的应用需明确的排序或交互建模关联，当前标题显示其为通用LLM智能体优化，与我的核心关注点关联较弱。

cs.CL
查看完整摘要
With external tools, large language models (LLMs) can tackle complex tasks through sustained environmental interaction. LLM-based agents are therefore increasingly used to tackle complex long-horizon tasks in real-world environments. However, these tasks involve sequential, interdependent decisions and changes in environmental states. Early execution errors affect subsequent interaction histories and environmental states, and their effects accumulate along the decision chain. To address this challenge, we propose Reflection-Guided Policy Optimization (ReflectPO), a reinforcement learning framework for autonomous error recovery in long-horizon tasks. ReflectPO comprises two core components. The first is Reflection-Guided Sampling. We use branch differences and repeated visits in interaction trajectories to construct hints for error recovery. The second is Tree-Structured Credit Assignment. We organize sampled trajectories into trees and assign action-level credit using parent--child reward differences and recovery gains. Experiments over two long-horizon tool-use benchmarks show that ReflectPO outperforms multiple existing baselines in most domains. It also achieves the best overall performance on average. Further analyses show that ReflectPO effectively improves agents' ability to recognize their own deviations, recover from erroneous states, and continue making progress toward task completion.

意义契约：大语言模型中的局部语义几何

3/10
Meaning Contracts: Local Semantic Geometry in Large Language Models
Andrés Daniel Godoy Ortiz
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究LLM内部的局部语义几何结构（意义契约），属于对LLM表征空间的可解释性/理论研究，与推荐、搜索、广告的直接应用距离较远。虽可能间接启发对语义嵌入空间的理解，但缺乏明确的RecSys/Search/Ads应用指向。

2026-10-22 04:32:30 | arXiv:2610.30715v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
This paper studies the local geometry of semantic meaning in large language models. Current representation analyses ask whether meanings are close in embedding space, or whether linear probes separate semantic classes, but they do not characterize the within-concept local geometry: what is invariant across realizations of a single semantic factor, what varies, and what changes when meaning is refined. This paper characterizes that geometry through two controlled probes evaluated on four architecturally diverse decoder-only LLMs (Gemma 4, Llama 3.2, Qwen 2.5, Pythia). A cross-lingual probe uses language as a perturbation to separate exact meaning matches, same-factor realizations, nearby concepts, and out-of-domain contrasts. A refinement probe separates specificity-bearing semantic narrowing from high-probability attributive elaboration and adversarial controls; both probes are validated by blind human judgments. Four findings recur across all models: under mean pooling, once dominant variance directions are removed, sentence pairs order by semantic relatedness (same factor, then same domain, then different domain) without overlap over a band of layers; semantic factors organize around an invariant core surrounded by structured realization-specific variation; refinement produces directed displacement into tighter neighborhoods, including against controls built on the same base noun; and sentence-level and token-local readouts expose complementary geometry (volume contraction at the sentence level, sharper anisotropic endpoints at token-local readout). We interpret these results as evidence that LLM semantics are organized as local anisotropic neighborhoods in which meaning contracts under refinement.

cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Singular-value spectra encode important structure in transformer feed-forward projections, yet their causal role in downstream behavior remains underexplored. We study post-hoc spectral smoothing and spiking interventions on the up_proj and down_proj matrices. Positive perturbation stretches deviations from the geometric mean, making large singular values larger and small ones smaller (spiking), and vice versa. The intervention preserves singular vectors and the geometric mean of the singular values. Across five open Llama and Qwen instruction models, we evaluate the perturbed systems on capability-labeled subtasks, standard and chain-of-thought reasoning benchmarks, judge-scored truthfulness, hallucination, and safety tasks, and TRAIT personality probes. To interpret score shifts, we fit responses in a three-dimensional capability basis consisting of factual knowledge, language understanding, and deductive reasoning. The induced changes suggest structured rather than purely random sensitivity: large positive perturbations more reliably suppress language and deductive responses, moderate negative perturbations can sometimes improve factual or task-local performance on the evaluated grids, and sensitivity varies strongly by model family, benchmark module, and underlying layerwise spectral inequality. These results position spectral perturbation as a lightweight intervention for probing how capabilities are organized and traded off in large language models.

2026-10-22 01:49:10 | arXiv:2610.30593v1 |
cs.DBcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Natural language querying for time series databases aims to let users find what they need in long histories of data by asking in plain language. Shape queries ask how a series moves. They are the main challenge of this task. Recent work makes them feasible by using symbolization as a bridge between the query and the long history. However, existing symbolizations were designed for compression, similarity indexing, or classification. What a symbolization should provide for natural language querying has not been systematically studied. We therefore examine the definitions of six classical symbolizations against this task and identify three limitations. (1) Direction is missing from the string, (2) direction is measured at too small a scale, and (3) the letters are arbitrary. Three design principles follow. We follow these principles and make a minimal redesign of SAX called TT-SAX (two-track SAX). The level letters stay. Each segment gains a trend letter. It is measured across neighboring segments rather than inside one, and graded to match the adjectives that queries use. We conduct extensive experiments on the public NLQTSBench benchmark, comparing TT-SAX with the six classical symbolizations across six LLMs. TT-SAX outperforms all six on shape queries, and ablations trace its gains back to the three limitations one by one. This paper is the first systematic study of time series symbolization with natural language querying as the design goal. It provides a foundation for future research on this task. Our code is available at https://github.com/Atlamtiz/tt-sax.

Joint-embedding predictive architectures (JEPAs) learn world models in latent space, enabling efficient planning but leaving their states opaque. We introduce Inside-JEPA, which places relational channels for ordered entity pairs inside the JEPA latent state and shows that an inverse-dynamics loss can ground these channels without relation labels when its head reads them, either alone or as part of the full state. On Something-Something V2, latent prediction alone performs no better than a random relation head, while inverse-dynamics supervision that recovers actions from channel changes improves relational AUROC by .065-.103 across ten settings with annotated boxes, by .081 on RH20T robot episodes with held-out tasks, and by .050-.061 across five relations using off-the-shelf detection and tracking. Long chain-of-thought reasoning improves vision-language models (VLMs), but it also exposes a temporal grounding failure: as generation unfolds, models may drift from image evidence while reinforcement learning still provides only a final-answer reward. We study this gap through visual revisit, a temporally localized reactivation of image evidence during reasoning. Across three VLM backbones, revisit peaks are predictive of correctness and causally linked to performance: masking them degrades accuracy more than masking non-peaks, while injecting revisit-like peaks into failed rollouts partially restores correct answers. Control analyses show that this signal is not explained by raw attention strength, uncertainty, logit margin, or hidden-state norm. We propose RECAP, a GRPO-compatible training method that turns detached revisit traces into a credit-assignment signal. 查看完整摘要
Visual perception should be iterative and task-directed, with representations refined in response to the question and the need for further processing. While Vision-Language Models (VLMs) increasingly support extended reasoning, their visual tokens are still typically acquired in a single, task-agnostic encoder pass. This creates an evidence-acquisition bottleneck: models may reason longer without refining the visual evidence needed to answer the question. We propose LoopLook, a lightweight encoder-side recurrent perception framework that equips pretrained VLMs with adaptive visual depth. LoopLook recurrently revisits a middle-to-late window of the vision encoder, injecting question representations at each loop to guide visual refinement. A learned stopping mechanism adaptively determines the recurrent depth, while a token-wise Mixture-of-Looks (MoL) combines representations from different loops, allowing each visual token to selectively integrate information acquired across recurrent steps. Across four backbones and eight benchmarks, LoopLook consistently improves performance over the baseline, yielding gains of up to 8.6% on MMStar and 8.1% on HallusionBench. It also better preserves task-relevant visual information under reduced input resolution, while increasing time to first token (TTFT) by only 5.1-6.5%. By bringing recurrence from reasoning back into perception, LoopLook transforms visual perception from a one-shot glimpse into an adaptive process of recurrent refinement.

2026-10-22 03:05:25 | arXiv:2610.30643v1 |
cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) requires agents to follow instructions and navigate to target locations. However, current VLN agents often suffer severe performance degradation in unseen environments due to distribution shifts. Test-Time Adaptation (TTA) offers a promising paradigm to bridge this gap, yet existing methods that rely on unsupervised entropy minimization can propagate errors through the trajectory, causing cascading failures in navigation. Recent methods mitigate this by incorporating human feedback, but they apply trajectory-level supervision uniformly across all steps without localizing the actions is truly informative, leading to inefficient feedback utilization and mismatched optimization granularity. To address these limitations, we propose FETA, a Feedback-Efficient Test-Time Adaptation method that improves both feedback efficiency and optimization reliability. To reduce unnecessary human interventions, we introduce a hierarchical sample selection strategy to localize informative samples. Specifically, we first filter trajectories whose normalized policy entropy exhibits large fluctuations. Within each selected trajectory, we further select the critical action via an entropy-gradient saliency criterion and query a single binary feedback on its correctness. A discrepancy-aware entropy optimization objective then updates the policy only at the queried step, with adaptive scaling that assigns larger relative weights to samples with greater prediction-feedback discrepancy. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that FETA achieves state-of-the-art performance with fewer human interventions, while remaining robust under noisy feedback and continual adaptation settings.

Developing and evaluating such policies requires faithful recommendation world models that capture both the interactive shopping environment and users' behavioral responses over long interactions. Existing resources rarely provide long-horizon, fine-grained real-user trajectories or support trajectory-level evaluation and offline validation against production A/B results. We introduce Lean Finder V2, a semantic search framework for Mathlib that disambiguates user queries through complementary model-level adversarial training and input-level query rewriting. Human mathematicians and coding agents rarely search Mathlib by providing detailed context. In practice, they query short properties, notation, aliases, or hypotheses, leaving much of the intended mathematics implicit. In contrast, existing Mathlib search engines unrealistically assume context-rich queries. The scarcity of real-world short queries paired with ground-truth Mathlib statements makes this ambiguity even more intractable. We argue that this is essentially a data problem. Through adversarial training, we scale up the generation of labeled short and ambiguous queries, and learn to disambiguate user queries at both model-level fine-tuning and input-level query rewriting. On the human-curated MathlibQR benchmark, our Lean Finder V2 sets a new state of the art, with at least 11% relative improvement over the previous best Mathlib semantic search engines. On top of our state-of-the-art retriever, optimized query rewriting further improves MathlibQR retrieval. On unlabeled queries from human and coding-agent interactions, Lean Finder V2 is ranked as the strongest system. Integrated with Claude Code, Lean Finder V2 improves proof success on downstream theorem-proving task. Lean Finder V2 is available at: https://leanfinder.github.io/

Test-time scaling has emerged as a key inference strategy for reasoning models, where a model generates a large number of candidate solutions in the hope of finding at least one correct solution. Running all candidate solutions to completion can be computationally demanding, so recent work prunes the candidate pool after generating only a short prefix, committing further computation only to the surviving candidates. Existing pruning strategies divide broadly into two categories: Quality-based methods assign a score to each partial solution and discard those predicted to ultimately lead to an incorrect final solution, while diversity-based methods select partial solutions that follow different reasoning trajectories and are therefore likely to cover different final solutions. The tension between these two objectives is an instance of exploration versus exploitation, yet little to no prior work on pruning exposes this trade-off directly, let alone optimizes it. In this paper, we introduce a pruning procedure that addresses this trade-off directly by optimizing pass@$k$ over the selected solution pool. In particular, it chooses the pool to maximize the probability that at least one of its partial solutions, when continued to completion, yields a correct answer. Because this objective simultaneously rewards high-quality partial solutions and penalizes redundancy among them, quality and diversity are naturally balanced by the criterion itself rather than through separate ad-hoc design choices. On eight open-weight reasoning models, our method outperforms existing quality-based and diversity-based pruning strategies. The gains are particularly pronounced when the pruning procedure keeps a moderate number of partial solutions for continued generation, highlighting the value of directly balancing quality and diversity during pruning.

面向基于LLM的IT工单分类的成本感知弃权机制

Cost-Aware Abstention for LLM-Based IT Ticket Classification
Sachin Gupta

cs.LGcs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Wrong automatic routing of an IT support ticket can cost more than human triage. We evaluate cost-aware abstention as an offline, cost-conditional readiness assessment: select a routing policy on calibration data under explicit costs, then evaluate that frozen policy once on held-out test data. On a public 47,837-ticket, eight-class corpus, we compare four trained non-LLM classifiers with four large language models under zero- and few-shot prompting. Under the illustrative cost model (c_ok, c_triage, c_wrong) = (0, 1, 5), TF-IDF with logistic regression and a threshold of 0.60 selected on calibration reduces test cost from 0.763 to 0.472, a 38.1% relative reduction (paired ratio-bootstrap 95% CI [35.7%, 40.4%]), while lowering wrong routes from 15.3% to 3.5%. Frozen calibration-selected policies also beat both always-route and always-defer on held-out test data in 14 of 16 settings in a post-hoc cost-parameter analysis. The strongest prompted configuration reaches 0.477 accuracy on a stratified 1,000-ticket test subset, versus 0.879 for fine-tuned DistilBERT on the same tickets. Isotonic lowers expected calibration error without reliably improving discrimination: GPT-4o-mini few-shot has one confidence value before and after recalibration and correctness AUROC 0.500. We therefore report correctness AUROC and tie-aware risk-coverage area alongside ECE. At the analytically derived break-even probability 0.80, seven prompted configurations defer all tickets; Haiku few-shot routes only 19/1,000. An LLM-only agreement control and margin/entropy rejection baselines further separate calibration from discrimination. The findings are scoped to this dataset, prompt design, model endpoints, and illustrative costs; the work is an evaluation framework, not a new abstention algorithm or universal deployment certification.

Multilingual pipelines such as FineWeb2 apply one recipe across a thousand languages, leaving the failure modes of individual languages unanticipated. We use an LLM once, as a corpus debugger rather than a classifier: a tool-using agent explores the corpus through a read-only, budgeted API and compiles each finding into an executable test, which a native speaker then validates. On the 26.5M-document Slovak portion of FineWeb2, three runs costing \$2.21-\$2.84 each found seven findings over four phenomena. The agent contributes exact prevalence, a precision estimate checked against sampled documents, an account of why the pipeline missed each slice, and a reusable test. We release the framework, Slovak test suite, validation data and agent trajectories.

Knowledge graph-to-text (KG-to-text) generation verbalizes a set of knowledge graph triples into fluent natural language. While fine-tuned encoder-decoder models have long been the standard approach, recent LLMs are becoming increasingly competitive. We benchmark recent LLMs against fine-tuned encoder-decoder models, using reference-based metrics and graph-grounded reference-free LLM-based evaluation validated against human annotations. cs.HCcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Systems that aim to help a user engage with viewpoints other than their own have to decide how different those viewpoints should be. Viewpoints are mainly represented on a scalar scale, too coarse to distinguish viewpoints that disagree with the user about different things. We introduce a selection policy in which a bandit sets how far viewpoints may sit from the user, and the policy selects the most novel viewpoint within that bound. We vary only its stance encoder, comparing a scalar score with Perspectivized Stance Vectors, which break a position into separate stances on concepts. We integrate it inside a dialog system and run a between-subjects study (N=63). Concept-level stance produces more varied exposure sequences and broader coverage of the topic's positions, but does not change how deeply users justify viewpoints or how accurately they reconstruct them. Finally, we analyze user--viewpoint relations at the concept level, showing that users reconstruct a viewpoint least accurately on concepts that one side took no position on, rather than on concepts where the two disagreed. Our findings open up opportunities for dialog systems that support engagement with alternative viewpoints by acting on which concepts a user and a viewpoint disagree on, rather than on distance alone.

Multilingual language models can give different identity judgments when the same people's names are written in different scripts. Qwen2.5-7B's same-person accuracy falls from 35.1% in Latin to 8.5% in Devanagari under Marathi prompts, while different-person accuracy remains above 98%. Museum description generation challenges this assumption because catalogue records are heterogeneous, partially visual, and unevenly documented across geographic regions. We study how metadata composition shapes the generation of artefact descriptions through a two-phase framework. Phase 1 analyses natural metadata variation in a large museum collection, where field count, field type, image availability, and Place co-vary.
