个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于成对评估方法在奖励模型/LLM对齐中的局限性，属于NLP评估与奖励建模的理论分析，而非RecSys/Search/Ads的核心技术。虽然奖励模型在推荐排序中偶有应用，但本文讨论的跨提示词奖励分数偏差缺乏明确的推荐/搜索/广告场景联系，属于较边缘的相关性。

2026-10-22 11:00:10 | arXiv:2610.31090v1 |
cs.CLcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Reward models score responses for preference optimization, filtering and ranking, and pooling scores across prompts needs them to be comparable. Yet pairwise evaluation compares two responses to the same prompt, so any score component shared by a prompt's responses cancels and goes untested. We study this component by comparing between-prompt variation in model scores with the same variation in human ratings of the same responses. We propose a human-referenced audit, built from a between-prompt variance share, a sibling-rating regression and prompt substitutions, and apply it to eighteen released scorers and six matched training conditions. We find that scores cluster by prompt more than the ratings do: on HelpSteer2, six reward models have between-prompt shares of 0.34 to 0.64 against a human share near zero. Replacing the original prompt with a fixed instruction keeps substantial clustering but weakens agreement with prompt-level human ratings, so clustering alone does not show agreement with humans. Part of that variation is useful: subtracting prompt means lowers the helpfulness of globally selected responses, and scores stay sensitive to a wrong prompt even when training shuffles prompt means. We propose that reward evaluation check between-prompt variation, sensitivity to a wrong prompt and usefulness for selection separately.

SCOPE：将自然语言策略编译为神经符号谓词程序

2/10
SCOPE: Compiling Natural-Language Policies into Neurosymbolic Predicate Programs
Arnon Mazza, Elad Levi
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于将自然语言策略编译为神经符号程序，属于程序合成与神经符号推理方向，与推荐系统、搜索或广告的核心技术（如排序、用户建模、特征交互）无直接关联。其涉及的自然语言策略解析与符号执行对RecSys/Ads排序策略自动化生成的潜在借鉴意义非常边缘，故相关性较低。

2026-10-22 10:41:13 | arXiv:2610.31062v1 |
cs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Policy enforcement for LLM agents has shifted from single-turn content moderation to compound, application-specific policies evaluated over multi-turn transcripts and tool-call trajectories. Fine-tuned guardrail models are efficient but degrade on compound policies, and while LLM-as-a-judge approaches achieve higher accuracy, they incur high inference cost and tend to produce unstable predictions. Neurosymbolic methods offer a natural alternative, decomposing the policy into narrow neural decisions composed under explicit logic. However, existing symbolic layers lack the required expressiveness and are optimized against fixed data rather than the policy itself. To address this, we introduce SCOPE, which compiles a policy into an executable program of neural predicates executed by small models, deterministic extractors, and code that composes their outputs. The program is optimized jointly with its training data, using adversarial generation of counterexamples that target where the program deviates from the policy. We evaluate SCOPE on a benchmark of real-world policy tasks against prompt optimization, task decomposition, and workflow generation methods, achieving the highest average accuracy across tasks and models. Programs optimized with the weakest executor transfer to other models without further optimization. This allows them to be deployed in production on inexpensive models, where they reach accuracy comparable to frontier reasoning models. Unlike these monolithic models, SCOPE produces substantially more stable predictions, and each verdict is interpretable through the predicate outputs that produced it. Additionally, we validate the key components of our method through an ablation study. SCOPE thus enables reliable, interpretable, and efficient policy enforcement at production scale.

Single-document LoRA adapters have been proposed as units of knowledge to be merged and retrieved; yet open-book scoring, with the document in the prompt, can credit a document's own adapter (its specialist) for reading that document as readily as for holding it. Training one adapter for each of 150 Wikipedia articles on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, we find that open-book scores mostly measure a reading behaviour every adapter shares: a random adapter trained on another document recovers 82 % of the specialist's open-book advantage over the base model but only 2 % of its advantage on a cloze probe that asks for the same document's facts without providing the source text, a share we cannot distinguish from zero. Content does reach the weights, since the specialists beat the base model in cloze recall by 0.094, and the geometry of their updates follows the documents they read; yet, in a test of 30 queries, adapters chosen by weight-space proximity or text embedding are not distinguishable from random ones on either probe, although that test cannot exclude an advantage of about four fifths of what a query's own adapter gains over a random one. At 16 documents or more, correctly scaled merges recover 87 % to 89 % of the specialists' open-book advantage but only 12 % to 16 % of their cloze advantage, with intervals that run from below zero to at most 32 %, against 94 % for joint training.We also report four silent errors, each of which produced a clean, wrong result.

填充至 k：Schema 中的最小数量使开放权重模型填充其抽取列表

Padding to k: A Minimum Count in the Schema Makes Open-Weight Models Pad Their Extraction Lists
Abhishek Barali, Aayush Paudel
个性化推荐理由:

2026-10-22 09:38:58 | arXiv:2610.30997v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Extraction pipelines ask language models for JSON that follows a schema, and the schema can set a minimum number of list entries. We ask what models do when the document holds fewer items than that minimum. Under a preregistered plan, we test eight open-weight models on 558 real documents from nine annotated datasets, with six more models on hosted services as a reference. Three findings stand out. First, models treat the count as a target: a minimum shown in the schema, but not enforced, raises the share of lists longer than the true count from 34.5% to 55.4%. Second, the padding is hard to catch, because most added entries are real text taken from the document; about half of them pass a check that each entry occurs in the source. Third, common fixes help only partly: a line saying fewer items are fine removes the effect on average but misses more true items, a count in prose ("up to k") also raises over-counting, an "N/A" option under an enforced minimum still leaves half the lists padded, and re-asking with the validation error makes padding far more common. We release the code, answer lists and run records at https://github.com/AbhishekBarali/padding-to-k

Once a Tree, Always a Tree? Cross-lingual Transfer of Hierarchical Generalization in Language Models
Shin Yamaguchi, Masaya Taniguchi, Tatsuki Kuribayashi, Taro Watanabe, Yoshinobu ...
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究语言模型在跨语言场景下对层次结构泛化能力的迁移，属于纯语言学/认知科学导向的LLM分析，未涉及推荐、搜索或广告中的排序、用户建模或多模态异质数据统一建模。其结论难以直接转化为RecSys/Search/Ads的模型改进或系统设计。

2026-10-22 09:07:44 | arXiv:2610.30960v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Language models (LMs) often exhibit successful cross-lingual transfer through continued (pre)training, still their underlying process remains unclear. One possibility is that LMs can leverage the evidence toward language-universal principles, namely, hierarchical structure, from a source language (L1) to a new language (L2), while many other non-linguistic explanations, e.g., improved initialization of position embeddings, can be potentially possible. In this study, to quantify the cross-linguistic transferability of linguistic evidence in cross-lingual LM training, we introduce a new experimental design, namely, a cross-lingual poverty-of-stimulus (CPOS) setting that ablates how LMs' hierarchical generalization in L2 can be led by structural evidence provided in L1. We contrast counterfactual L1 and L2 learning scenarios where the L2 corpus is ambiguous between linear and hierarchical grammar rules, while the amount of positive evidence for the hierarchical rule is controlled on the L1 side. Our experimental results show that LMs can partially, yet not perfectly, transfer L1 structural evidence to L2, yielding only a small but detectable hierarchical bias, while the strength of transfer depends on language pairs. We further ablate the conditions for successful transfer, motivated by recent reports on the substantial impact of vocabulary overlap. We indeed find that vocabulary overlap is crucial even for the cross-lingual transfer of hierarchical syntactic generalization. This suggests that this also explains the language-pair-dependent tendencies in the main experiments and suggests that the transfer of linguistic evidence in continued new-language learning is only conditionally achievable.

Andrea Blasi Núñez, Gianluca Barmina, Federico Torrielli, Thiusius Rajeeth Savar...

2026-10-22 08:54:58 | arXiv:2610.30934v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Activation verbalizers translate the internal representations of large language models into human-readable text. The recently proposed natural language autoencoders (NLAs) learn this by reconstructing activations through a natural language bottleneck. However, it remains unclear how well these methods generalize beyond the setting they were trained for. Here, we study this question for NLAs along four closely related questions: (i) Do NLA verbalizations exhibit compositional structure with respect to their input? (ii) Do NLAs generalize to finetuned versions of the model they were trained for? (iii) Can NLAs recover finetuning objectives in model diffing? (iv) Can NLAs be specialized for model diffing? Across four models from three families, our experiments provide evidence supporting a largely positive answer to all four questions. Specifically, verbalizations of composed analogy activations mostly name the target while rarely mentioning the subtracted word, and an NLA can be reused on lightweight finetunes of its original model without retraining, with reconstruction quality degrading noticeably only for the largest model. Verbalizing activation differences between Taboo model organisms and their original models recovers the concealed secret word for most organisms, and reinforcement learning on activation differences further improves this recovery on held-out organisms. We also find that NLAs can recover the finetuning objective without the finetuned model generating any token, since the most informative token positions precede the user prompt. NLAs do not yet match state-of-the-art model diffing methods on the emergent misalignment detection task, but outperform a blackbox baseline. Our findings suggest that NLAs can meaningfully verbalize differences in activation space, rendering NLAs a promising direction for future work.

2026-10-22 08:22:50 | arXiv:2610.30900v1 |
cs.CLcs.CY
查看完整摘要
Silicon sampling generates survey respondents by having a large language model (LLM) answer a survey as a person with given demographic attributes, and the resulting synthetic populations are increasingly used in place of human ones. Existing evaluations focus on individual-answer accuracy, per-question answer distributions, and correlations across questions. None of these compares the respondents themselves, so a generator whose respondents have combinations of answers found in no human respondent can still score well if it reproduces every answer distribution. We propose the respondent coverage gap (RCG), which represents each respondent as a point defined by their complete answer profile and measures how far each human respondent is from the nearest generated one, relative to the distance between human samples. On two US social surveys, all six LLMs we evaluate leave human respondents uncovered, and under our main settings, they fall behind a baseline that samples each answer independently from the human marginals. Across the prompting and inference settings we test, every LLM has a higher RCG than a Gaussian copula fitted to the human data.

一位教师，多种视角：降低在线策略自蒸馏中的参考敏感性

2/10
One Teacher, Many Views: Reducing Reference Sensitivity in On-Policy Self-Distillation
Khouloud Saadi, Di Wang
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于LLM自蒸馏训练中参考模型敏感性的降低，属于纯模型训练/优化技术，未直接涉及推荐、搜索或广告场景。虽然蒸馏技术理论上可用于压缩排序模型，但其核心贡献（在线策略自蒸馏的参考敏感性）与RecSys/Search/Ads的关联非常间接，缺乏明确的应用潜力。

2026-10-22 07:59:48 | arXiv:2610.30880v1 |
cs.LGcs.CL
查看完整摘要
On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) improves reasoning by training a model on its own trajectories with dense token-level supervision from a privileged copy conditioned on a reference solution. Existing methods typically use a single teacher distribution conditioned on the complete reference. We identify a limitation of this formulation: a reference is only one realization of the reasoning process. Although it provides evidence about the answer, it also encodes an ordering, phrasing, and solution path. Full-reference conditioning can therefore produce correct targets that depend excessively on information unavailable to the student at inference. We show that replacing one valid reference with another, while holding the problem and student state fixed, can substantially alter the teacher distribution, a phenomenon we call reference-induced target instability. To address this instability, we introduce Monte Carlo Privileged Distillation (MCPD), which models privileged context as a distribution over partial reference views and distills the marginal teacher distribution. MCPD evaluates the teacher under multiple views and averages the resulting next-token distributions. We show that this probability-space average is the exact target induced by minimizing the expected teacher-to-student KL divergence over privileged views. Across mathematical and scientific reasoning benchmarks, we show that MCPD produces more reference-consistent supervision and consistently improves over full-reference OPSD.

Firoj Paudel

2026-10-22 07:41:21 | arXiv:2610.30860v1 |
cs.CLcs.SD
查看完整摘要
Nepali-English code-switched conversational speech presents severe challenges for automatic speech recognition (ASR), chiefly through script confusion (inappropriate mixing of Devanagari and Latin orthography) and elevated error rates at language switch points. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) can adapt multilingual foundation models, but human code-switched audio is expensive to collect and annotate. We curate a new benchmark of 13,044 spontaneous podcast utterances (15.45 h total; 10,214 test, 2,830 dev), audited and verified to eliminate machine translation leakages and acoustic loops. We investigate a two-stage synthetic-to-real adaptation framework: Stage 1 applies SFT on 0.95 h of speech synthesized via a multi-speaker, dual-engine TTS pipeline; Stage 2 explores Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with transcription and script rewards. We find that synthetic SFT provides the primary cross-domain transfer to spontaneous human speech, reducing Mixed Error Rate (MER) from 23.40% to 21.52% (-1.88 pp) and switch-point error rate (SPER) from 35.89% to 28.77% (-7.12 pp). Subsequent GRPO yields small, statistically significant adjustments on 173 test utterances (1.69%), driving English script confusion (SCR_en) from 6.45% to 6.36% (p <= 0.001) and SPER to 28.70% (p = 0.022 clustered). Crucially, ablation reveals that in this SFT-converged encoder-decoder setting, an auxiliary script reward term yields performance identical to MER-only optimization because rollout candidate groups exhibit near-zero script variance on synthetic data. On monolingual benchmarks, SFT improves Nepali MER by 2.71 pp (stabilizing at 18.42% after GRPO, a 2.38 pp gain over baseline) while preserving English lexical WER (3.38% vs. 3.33%). Verified transcripts, timestamps, episode metadata, and reconstruction scripts are publicly released.

TRIAD: Benchmarking Omni-Modal Ambiguity in Multimodal Large Language Models
Zhaolu Kang, Yidi Wang, Yile Li, Siqi Zeng, Yingjie He, Richeng Xuan, Zhichao hu
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于多模态大模型歧义推理的基准与评测，属于纯评估类工作，并非我的核心关注点（核心推荐/搜索/广告进展、LLM使能技术、Transformer架构或直接LLM应用）。其基准本身对RecSys/Search/Ads缺乏直接的方法或架构贡献，比较价值有限。

2026-10-22 07:15:40 | arXiv:2610.30841v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Ambiguity is a central feature of natural communication: a sentence, image, or sound may remain underspecified until evidence from the other channels is considered. Yet current multimodal benchmarks mostly test perception, recognition, or reasoning over already determinate inputs, leaving unclear whether omni-modal large language models can resolve ambiguity distributed across text, vision, and audio. We introduce TRIAD, a benchmark for omni-modal ambiguity resolution. Each item is a text--image--audio triplet paired with a question and a set of answer options, and is constructed so that the full triplet determines a unique answer while removing any one modality makes the set of consistent options non-unique. TRIAD contains $327$ hand-written queries in $86$ scene groups and covers $18$ ambiguity categories across the three modalities. Evaluating $14$ omni-modal systems reveals that strong item-level performance does not translate into robust scene-level disambiguation: the best model still trails humans by over $20$ points in group accuracy. Removing answer options widens the gap further, driving model group accuracy to the single digits while humans remain robust. Leave-one-out and transcript/description counterfactuals further show that current systems often underuse audio cues when text and image are present. TRIAD reframes omni-modal evaluation around disambiguation rather than recognition, and provides a taxonomy, benchmark, and diagnostic protocol for measuring this capability.

Invisibilization as a Source of Probability Bias in Language Models
Christian Arnold, Ernesto Calvo, Guadalupe Gonzalez, Sebastián Vallejo Vera, Tia...

2026-10-22 06:03:35 | arXiv:2610.30785v1 |
cs.CLcs.CY
查看完整摘要
Language models are aligned to avoid using protected attributes such as race, ethnicity, and gender to inform their responses. Therefore, they increasingly refuse or hedge when such attributes appear in a prompt. However, if a system must decide how much to learn from what people tell it, it is costly to refuse valuable information because of its source. In this article, we measure the cost of declining information in a two-urn game with an exact Bayesian answer. We present leading LLM models from two developers (OpenAI and Anthropic) with a prompt that requires them to decide how far to trust an unverifiable report from a participant whose description we randomize across ten configurations (6,600 sessions). We show that leading models often decline to assess the reporter, treating information as worthless at rates set by the description rather than by the evidence. At its widest, models decline a Black-described reporter 46% of the time and a white-described reporter 9%; yet, when they do assess the information, the Black-described reporter receives slightly more trust. The bias is invisible to audits that compare average trust or look only at assessed cases. It grows with reasoning effort, follows words rather than categories (models invoke race for "Black" but not for its Spanish translation negro, and never for "Indigenous"), and worsens under warning instructions a practitioner would try first. We also present a scoring option that reduces the refusal rate bias. When a model's trust is scored against revealed truth over repeated rounds, it credits identical records equally. In four of five configurations from Anthropic and OpenAI, the racial gap in trust falls below 0.02 by the last rounds.

cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Do people who speak different languages organize concepts in similar ways? This question lies at the heart of the Sapir--Whorf hypothesis and debates about linguistic relativity. Shared basic meanings, however, need not imply shared relations among them. We compared relations among 196 basic concepts across 81 languages, using word embeddings from two multilingual models to quantify conceptual structure expressed in language. Cross-language correspondence was greater for sociocultural meanings than for meanings concerning nature and motion, a contrast recurring across models and classifications. Within the sociocultural domain, relational differences increased with geographic separation; less closely related languages differed more across all domains. These findings suggest that universality and relativity are interwoven across conceptual domains. They inform an account in which shared cognitive resources interact with linguistic and local experience, and predict greater cross-language correspondence in human semantic judgments for sociocultural than for nature and motion meanings.

cs.HCcs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
AI assistants built on large language models (LLMs) are trained on human feedback and increasingly personalized to users' stated preferences. Yet how closely these preferences reflect what people reward in responses remains unclear. We compare stated and revealed preferences in the public PRISM dataset, in which 1,396 participants reported how much seven response attributes mattered to them and then rated 29,347 responses from 21 LLMs. Among responses to the same prompt, features the survey never asked about, such as response length, formatting and refusal language, were at least as strongly associated with ratings as text proxies of the surveyed attributes. Whether stated priorities matched ratings depended on how attributes were measured: participants who prioritized an attribute favoured responses from models that others perceived as strong on it, but not responses that scored higher on text proxies for it. Written feedback was more telling: participants who asked for shorter answers, even in earlier conversations, gave length less weight in their ratings, and those asking for longer answers gave it more. Their relative priorities over the surveyed attributes, however, did not predict this weight. Reasons given for choices reflected perceived attributes more than measured features. Participants also differed stably in how much they weighted several concrete features, especially length, but with a median of 6 conversations per participant, these weights were estimated poorly. The largest gain from personal information in predicting held-out choices was 0.13 percentage points, compared with 1.11 for a stronger pooled model. Stated priorities thus track perceived attributes of AI responses but say little about the concrete features that ratings reward, a distinction that matters for preference elicitation and AI personalization.

cs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
When a multilingual LLM agrees with a user's false premise, is it being sycophantic, or does it simply not know better in that language? Current research does not distinguish these cases: they record only whether the model agreed, not why. We introduce the SC-matrix, that crosses a model's competence (whether it answers correctly under no pressure) with its sycophancy (whether it maintains that answer under pressure). Our results show that (1) identical aggregate sycophancy rates can mask different failure compositions, (2) high competence in a language does not always protect against sycophancy, and (3) reasoning-oriented mitigations outperform directive-based strategies. We find that multilingual sycophancy is not a single phenomenon but a composite of different failures that require distinct diagnosis.

Existing datasets on cultural knowledge have been typically created by human annotators familiar with each culture. However, the process requires considerable human effort, presenting a significant challenge to scalability. To address this challenge, we introduce WikiOriginQA, a QA dataset automatically constructed by objectively representing the origins of knowledge through the creation dates of different Wikipedia language editions. Experimental results with the created dataset confirm that LLM performance tends to be biased toward Anglophone cultures. These findings demonstrate the validity of our automated approach, enabling cultural bias evaluations with superior scalability across diverse languages without extensive manual annotation.

Our resulting model, SunflowerASR, achieves the best published macro-averaged WER on the AfriVox-v2 (0.227) and SimbaBench (0.372) benchmarks, and is publicly available.

What the Specification Did Not Say: Specification-Oracle Gaps in Test-Graded Coding-Agent Benchmarks
Fei Ding
个性化推荐理由:

该论文研究编码智能体基准中规范描述与测试预言机（ground-truth判断）之间的语义缺口，属于LLM智能体评测与基准构建的方法论分析，与推荐、搜索、广告系统无直接关联。虽然其中关于规范歧义影响评测有效性的思想可能对RecSys/Ads中LLM智能体评测设计有微弱启发，但整体属于纯NLP/软件工程评测范畴，不符合当前关注方向。

2026-10-22 02:56:44 | arXiv:2610.30635v1 |
cs.CL
查看完整摘要
Coding-agent benchmarks often describe a task in natural language but grade it with tests taken from the upstream project. Passing those tests can require information the description never states: a constant's name, a module path, an exact message. We call this a specification-oracle gap: where it exists, a benchmark also measures whether an agent reproduces upstream implementation details. We formalise the gap, including the inconsistencies that chains of dependent tasks add, and build a static audit for five languages that classifies every name-, path- and literal-level dependency of every scored test (held-out precision 89.2%, recall 92.9%). On SWE-Milestone (98 graded milestones, 35 published runs), 25 milestones contain a test that depends on an unstated, non-inferable name or path. Build units with such a dependency fail in 80.6% of runs, against 48.4% for comparable units without one, and withholding a single hidden name from the reference stops its unit's tests from compiling in 126 of 128 cases. The leaderboard's ranking is robust to removing the affected tests, but they shift scores within repositories and favour agents that reproduce upstream names. Across four further benchmarks, prevalence appears to follow construction: a third or more of the tasks are affected where specifications are release notes or LLM summaries, almost none where tasks are screened or written for their tests; how much a gap costs depends on how the harness grades. We release the audit, corrected filter lists and specification addenda.

Governed Self-Evolution for Autonomous Investing: A Multi-Agent LLM System with an Invariant Core
Yebo Feng, Jingyang Dai, Liangrui Pan, Nelson Lau, Jiahua Xu, Yang Liu

cs.AIcs.CLcs.MAq-fin.PM
查看完整摘要
LLM agents that act on live Web data must combine heterogeneous evidence, remember what they have done and improve from outcomes without weakening their own safeguards. We study this problem in autonomous investing, where every action is irreversible and based on untrusted Web content. We present Steward, a multi-agent LLM system that draws the boundary of self-evolution in its type system. First, nine role-specialised agents decide jointly: four analysts read news feeds, SEC filings, macroeconomic series and market data and emit typed reports, from which a Portfolio Manager sets target positions that a protected Risk Manager audits. Second, a seven-layer shared memory, assembled by code from the system's own record, gives each decision its position context, the market narrative, calibration and the agent's past decisions. Third, the system improves itself by review: code grades every trade and forecast nightly and writes the grades into memory, and each quarter a reflector proposes prompt learnings that a deterministic gate applies reversibly and replay of recorded inputs verifies. All three operate around an invariant core of order arithmetic, risk rules and discipline-agent prompts that no self-edit can reach. In a five-month live deployment (102 trading days, 1,632 LLM calls), the system contained a 24.6% sector drawdown to a 0.3% loss; in replay of its recorded inputs, a single model given the same raw data traded more and lost 1.35 points against the market over five days where the joint design showed no significant loss, and no injected instruction was followed; and a 2015-2026 backtest shows the core alone lowering the maximum drawdown under all 50 random entry policies. The reflector traced the dominant failure to its root cause, and the gate applied a learning that the agent adopted on trigger evenings and on no control evening. 聚合分数背后：系统性地改变 Arena-Hard 的 LLM 评判器揭示了什么

2/10
Behind Aggregate Scores: What Systematically Varying Arena-Hard's LLM Judge Reveals
Louise Yorke
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于 LLM 评判器（judge）的评估方法论与基准可靠性，属于纯 NLP 评测/基准研究范畴，而非面向推荐、搜索或广告的核心技术。它既不涉及 RecSys/Search/Ads 的建模、排序或 Transformer 架构创新，也不属于可迁移到这些领域的 LLM 使能技术（如效率、注意力、MoE 等）。因此与当前关注方向相关性很低。

2026-10-21 22:46:14 | arXiv:2610.30512v1 |
cs.CLcs.AIcs.CY
查看完整摘要
LLM judges are used to determine which of two answers is better. Trusting these verdicts requires knowing how repeatable they are and how much they depend on the judge's instructions. We show that similar aggregate scores can conceal substantial changes in individual verdicts, both when the same setup is repeated and when the instructions change. Using 500 Arena-Hard v2.0 prompts with two models' answers held fixed, we run GPT-4.1 as the judge under all eight combinations of criteria, output format and task framing, and repeat every setup unchanged at temperature zero on a pinned model version. Rerunning the default setup moves the aggregate score by about one point but changes the win/tie/loss outcome on 21.2% of prompts. Instruction changes introduce further sensitivity: in one setup, which asks for the verdict without reasoning and also drops several other instructions, outcomes change on 28.2 percentage points more prompts than the two setups' unchanged reruns do on average, and the aggregate score falls by 4.09 points. In another, where the criteria prioritise accuracy, outcomes change on 6.7 percentage points more prompts by the same comparison, while the aggregate score barely moves. A stable aggregate score therefore does not establish that individual verdicts are repeatable. This distinction matters when those verdicts are used as preference labels or to assess individual responses. Evaluations that rely on individual verdicts should repeat each setup they use and report both aggregate scores and prompt-level agreement.

Large language models (LLMs) are widely used to answer factual questions, but their answers can contain errors and must be fact-checked claim by claim. Yet the resulting score depends on three design decisions: which questions the claims answer, under what conditions each claim is judged, and which sources count as evidence. No existing benchmark specifies all three. We introduce JSFactCheckBench, a Japanese benchmark that specifies all three, with 1,913 claims from LLM answers to questions about misleading information that circulated on Japanese social media, each annotated for factuality at a reference time and for importance to the question, using evidence from preselected Web sources. These weaknesses are visible only because the benchmark records the conditions behind each label. A benchmark that leaves them implicit cannot reveal them.

cs.LGcs.AIcs.CL
查看完整摘要
Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) were originally motivated by discovering monosemantic features, a property that characterizes individual features but does not specify what the learned feature set should look like as a whole. As SAEs have increasingly been adopted for attribution, circuit discovery, and intervention, researchers have begun evaluating metrics that describe global properties of the feature set, such as sparsity, dead feature rates, reconstruction fidelity, and disentanglement, a diverse set that extends far beyond monosemanticity. However, this implicit shift beyond monosemanticity has not been formalized as a clear, well-defined objective that comes before these evaluation metrics. Without such an objective, existing metrics remain ad hoc, with their necessity unjustified. In this work, we formalize this ultimate objective by introducing a new paradigm for SAE evaluation: requiring that SAE features, and more generally dictionary-based decompositions of model representations, form an atomic interpretable unit basis, a coordinate system for semantically meaningful explanation. Under this paradigm, we organize existing and new measurements such as DAME (Dominance-Aware Monosemanticity Evaluation) as proxy metrics and introduce a comprehensive benchmarking framework. Evaluating hundreds of SAEs across multiple architectures and model families, we uncover failure modes in which some SAEs appear monosemantic under existing evaluation metrics while remaining functionally polysemantic: the most dominant features, those contributing most to model computation, are systematically less interpretable and more redundant. These failure modes warrant attention from SAE researchers as targets for improved training and architectures, and motivate dominance-aware evaluation as a standard check for practitioners before SAE features are deployed for attribution, circuit tracing, or intervention.

2026-10-22 17:26:34 | arXiv:2610.31592v1 |
cs.CVcs.LG
查看完整摘要
Diffusion models have become strong priors for solving inverse problems. However, nonlinear inverse problems such as phase retrieval remain challenging when multiple measurement-consistent solutions are distinguishable only by the prior; we call such measurements ambiguous. Existing diffusion-based samplers rely on local approximations during reconstruction. For an ambiguous measurement, these approximations can allow measurement guidance to favor a measurement-consistent mode that is close to a prior-supported mode but is itself not supported by the prior. Evaluation often masks this type of failure by reporting the best-of-$k$ runs, where the best is selected by comparison to the ground truth. We introduce Prior-Consistent DAPS (PC-DAPS), a variant of Decoupled Annealing Posterior Sampling (DAPS) designed to address this failure case by maintaining multiple candidate solutions within a single sampling run. As annealing noise decreases, PC-DAPS prunes and replaces weaker candidates according to their prior consistency, without any ground-truth access. PC-DAPS reduces the incidence of prior-inconsistent samples and improves sample quality on nonlinear inverse problems while preserving sample quality and diversity on linear inverse problems.

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have shown strong potential for video anomaly detection, but dense frame-level inference makes them impractical for real-time deployment. Existing acceleration commonly relies on uniform sampling, which ignores content dynamics and can either waste computation on static segments or miss localized anomalies. We propose MoG-VLM a motion-adaptive anomaly detection framework that leverages motion purely as a lightweight control signal to decide when VLM inference is necessary. To this end, we introduce Temporal Gradient Magnitude (TGM) which performs content-aware sampling to concentrate computation on informative regions while avoiding redundant processing. On XD-Violence, we improve over the previous best result by +0.57% while delivering a 1.31x inference speedup. These results demonstrate that content-aware allocation of VLM capacity, processing fewer frames more intelligently rather than all frames uniformly, is a more effective axis of progress than uniformly densifying VLM inference across frames. Many questions about a video concern one physical object. Where does the ball end up after the cups are shuffled? Which card is the queen? How often was the mug covered? Video-language models (Video-LMs) recognise such objects in single frames but do not keep them apart over time. On real shell-game videos, open models from 8B to 235B parameters, their thinking variants, a reinforcement-learned tool agent and three training-free tool agents all stay within ten points of chance. We separate semantic identity binding from temporal identity maintenance. An object's identity should be fixed at a moment when the evidence for it is visible, and carried afterwards by a tracker. \emph{Track, Then Bind} implements this principle without training. A promptable video tracker follows every candidate object and draws it with a track number. A frozen vision-language model answers a static question about the identifying evidence, which binds the target to a track. With Qwen3-VL-32B it solves both VET-Bench tasks, where a fine-tuned trajectory model reaches 92\% and 90\% and the same VLM voting over 16 samples stays at 30--32\%. Drawing the same tracks on the video and asking the question directly falls 12--19 points short of the agent on real cup games.

MixPT: Point Transformer with Interleaved Window and Global Attention
Minkyun Seo, Hyungtae Lim, Jaesik Park
个性化推荐理由:

2026-10-22 15:49:28 | arXiv:2610.31455v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Point cloud transformers aim to capture detailed local geometry and relationships across a scene at a manageable computational cost. Two common approaches are window attention and global attention. They differ in how they enable scene-wide interaction, which refers to information exchange among tokens across a scene. Window attention reduces computational cost by restricting self-attention to groups of tokens called windows; in serialized designs, each window contains a fixed number of consecutive tokens from a spatially ordered sequence. Because each block only directly connects tokens within the same window, information exchange across windows relies on changes in window membership across blocks and hierarchical pooling. Global attention, in contrast, directly connects all tokens within a scene, but computing attention over all token pairs can be costly when the number of tokens is large. To balance computational cost and scene-wide interaction, we introduce MixPT, a point transformer that uses a mix of window and global attention blocks in an interleaved schedule, while retaining a single fixed serialization pattern for its window blocks. For scenes with a large number of tokens, MixPT applies global attention to spatially pooled tokens and returns residual updates to the original-resolution stream. Across indoor and outdoor benchmarks, MixPT achieves competitive accuracy with lower latency than representative window and global attention baselines. We further demonstrate the feasibility of scaling the attention stage in depth by repeating the interleaved pattern of window and global attention blocks.

cs.ROcs.CV
查看完整摘要
A conventional embodied agent in an indoor environment builds a map by accumulating noisy real-world measurements and results from heavy inference to guide navigation. While the detailed measurements may differ due to scene changes or sensor settings, the unique topological structure of navigable passages or the connectivity between rooms may provide training-free guidance that complements local observations. We propose a sparse topometric graph as an efficient structural representation for robust indoor navigation, alleviating the need for fine-grained matching, which is computationally expensive, memory-intensive, and prone to real-world variations. The map consists of nodes that encode local 3D geometry and distance-weighted edges that capture traversable connectivity. A learned graph-matching network finds consistent subgraph structures across sessions by considering both node-level geometric descriptors and neighborhood connectivity. The holistic contextual matching is robust to local ambiguities caused by weak distinctiveness or repetitive structure, and establishes stable correspondences despite local scene changes, different sensor modalities, or limited spatial overlap. We analyze the practicality of the graph through cross-session point-goal navigation, where a goal specified in a prior reference map must be reached from an unknown initial pose in a later session. Experiments show that our method achieves the highest success rates (77.1% on Gibson and 40.0% under 3D-FRONT scene changes), while reducing memory by more than two orders of magnitude compared to point-level representations. The proposed approach further supports cross-session alignment and goal-directed navigation with strong scalability and robust real-world transfer.

Local feature matching is typically formulated for image pairs, although structure-from-motion and visual localization commonly match one reference image against many retrieved or covisible neighbours. Applying a pairwise matcher independently to these centre-neighbour edges duplicates reference-side computation and prevents the matcher from exploiting complementary multi-view geometric evidence from the remaining observations. To address these limitations, we introduce MVLoMa, a sparse star-graph matcher that extends LoMa from image pairs to multiple views. MVLoMa first uses a frozen multi-view geometry model to jointly describe all images in a star, then injects the resulting cross-view geometric context into local descriptors. It then matches the centre image to all neighbours in a single forward pass through per-view self-attention and bidirectional centre-neighbour cross-attention. For supervision, we derive positive correspondences only from depth-valid, mutually visible dense warps and explicitly penalize high-confidence matches that violate epipolar geometry. On 25-view star graphs from HyperSim, ScanNet++, Mip-NeRF 360, and Tanks and Temples, MVLoMa obtains the best macro-average relative-pose AUC among the compared methods at all three thresholds (58.65/85.41/90.99). Its matcher is 2.20 times faster than sequential evaluation of the 24 centre-neighbour edges. The same design supports four tested frozen geometry encoders -- DA3, $π^3$, VGGT, and VGGT-$Ω$ -- with DA3-Giant performing best on all reported encoder-ablation metrics. Controlled ablations further isolate the effects of geometry-aware descriptors, visibility filtering, geometric negative supervision, and joint multi-view inference.

4D scene reconstruction requires robust handling of dynamic objects, yet existing streaming methods commonly assume static scenes, leading to pose drift and ghosting artifacts over long sequences. We introduce Stream4D, a training-free, motion-decoupled streaming reconstruction framework that can be directly integrated into causal Transformer backbones (e.g., StreamVGGT, Stream3R). Our key observation is that causal attention implicitly encodes rich temporal motion cues within the KV cache. We adaptively aggregate these cues from multiple attention layers and across temporal windows, and fuse them with depth priors to handle challenging cases such as occlusions and textureless regions, producing precise dynamic masks that decouple static and dynamic regions. We then apply a soft key masking scheme that leverages these dynamic masks to suppress interference from dynamic regions in causal attention, thereby improving camera pose estimation accuracy. Stream4D inherits the efficiency of causal attention and remains compatible with existing streaming architectures. On diverse dynamic-scene benchmarks, Stream4D significantly improves camera pose estimation and static-scene reconstruction quality, yields reliable dynamic masks, and effectively mitigates long-sequence ghosting.

However, with the advent of the Vision-Language Models (VLMs) era, constrained by substantial computational resource demands, the mainstream research paradigm has shifted towards adapting general-purpose VLMs using RS vertical domain data for fine-tuning, rather than modifying their core architectures. Online semantic 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables open-vocabulary scene understanding from streaming observations. However, existing methods recover geometric context missing from image-derived semantic features using an additionally learned 3D network, incurring extra training and runtime overhead. We observe that such geometric-semantic evidence may already exist in the agent's observation history and can be retrieved rather than learned. Based on this insight, we introduce RecallGS (Geometry-Grounded Recall for Online Semantic Gaussian Splatting), a lightweight, training-free mechanism that treats past observations as a non-parametric geometric-semantic prior. RecallGS follows a recall-ground-fuse pipeline: CLIP retrieves relevant observations, alpha-compositing weights identify geometrically supported views, and aggregate their semantic features over each Gaussian's rendered footprint. On ScanNet and ScanNet++, RecallGS achieves strong semantic segmentation performance at 2.48 FPS, demonstrating that geometric-semantic context can be effectively recovered from observation history without an additional learned 3D branch.

查看完整摘要
Long egocentric videos pose a central challenge for multimodal language models: the visual stream must be compressed into a memory representation that can be searched efficiently while still preserving the evidence needed for question answering. Building on an online textual-memory framework in which first-person videos are converted into temporally grounded captions, this work systematically studies the role of retrieval in episodic-memory video question answering. We evaluate how different retrieval units, query formulations, context budgets, and reranking strategies affect multiple-choice answering on QAEgo4D-Closed, isolating retrieval as the main variable while keeping the underlying generated memory fixed. Our best configuration, Parent, matches fine-grained caption subchunks, then provides their full parent captions to the reasoner. It achieves 56.4 % accuracy, matching strong full-memory and retrieval baselines while exposing only a small fraction of the textual memory at question time. More importantly, our analysis shows that better temporal coverage does not necessarily translate into better answers: increasing the retrieval budget improves temporal Hit@K and Recall@K, but can reduce accuracy by introducing visually plausible distractors and uncertain caption evidence. These findings suggest that retrieval-augmented egocentric memory should not be optimized only for evidence recall, but also for the reliability, specificity, and answer-discriminative value of the retrieved context. The study motivates more structured episodic memories that move beyond raw caption retrieval toward evidence-aware representations for long-form egocentric reasoning.

Limited tumour visibility on intra-operative images complicates surgical navigation, including liver hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) targeting on computed tomography (CT) imaging. This makes labelled datasets difficult to curate for deep learning, and transferring pre-operative delineations through registration remains challenging due to intra-operative deformation and lack of paired pre- and intra-operative data. We propose to transfer tumour appearances learned from pre-operative magnetic resonance (MR) images to guide localisation on intra-operative CT through weak supervision. Building virtual embodied agents that operate in open-ended games like human players is a long-standing goal requiring both long-horizon autonomy and reliable native-interface control. As a procedurally generated open world, Minecraft provides a natural testbed for studying the integration of these capabilities. Existing agents typically provide either native visuomotor control without long-horizon autonomy or high-level planning executed through environment APIs or predefined skills. To bridge this gap, we propose HiSTEVE, a hierarchical VLA agent with a capability-aware agent harness. At the orchestration level, we equip the harness with structured memory for tracking progress and a VLM planner for selecting subtasks within the VLA's capabilities. cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Detailed image captioning is usually framed as an amount problem: a model should mention more visual facts while avoiding hallucination. However, autoregressive VLMs generate captions as ordered sequences, and later generations may be produced under weaker or noisier visual grounding. We therefore argue that long-form captioning should be studied not only by what facts are mentioned, but also by where they appear. We introduce a position-aware rubric representation that decomposes dense human references into atomic visual claims, verifies generated captions against those claims, and induces a quartile-wise composition of supported, hallucinated, and generic content. This lens reveals two qualitatively different failures in long captions: models tend to produce unsupported specificity in the middle, while the final portion often escapes into vague, non-evidential language rather than simply accumulating more hallucination. A same-reference human control does not exhibit these patterns; additional human audits examine the visual validity of the induced labels. Motivated by this diagnosis, we propose PAGER (Position-Aware Grounded Evidence Reinforcement), a GRPO training objective with separate signals for supported fine-grained middle evidence, evidence-bearing tail continuation, claim coverage, and content-aware length control. The result is a closed loop: the same ordered claim representation identifies where long captions fail and supplies the position-specific reward needed to repair those failures. Experiments on detailed-caption benchmarks show that PAGER improves position-wise grounding and pairwise caption preference while preserving competitive global caption quality.

Multimodal sentiment analysis (MSA) aims to infer sentiment by integrating linguistic, acoustic, and visual information. However, many fusion methods integrate modality-specific representations and cross-modal interactions into joint representations, making it difficult to determine whether predictive utility arises from a modality itself or from its interactions with others. More fundamentally, a modality's standalone predictive utility may not faithfully reflect its interaction-conditioned predictive utility. To address this issue, we propose I$^2$-KAN, an interpretable multi-pathway framework that explicitly maintains intra-modal and pairwise inter-modal information in separate structural pathways. Each pathway is refined by a Task-Decoupled Linear-Gated Denoising Bottleneck (TDL-GDB) to learn compact and task-relevant representations, followed by structured Kolmogorov-Arnold Network fusion that retains explicit pathway associations and supports multi-level interpretation. Extensive experiments on CMU-MOSI, CMU-MOSEI, and CH-SIMS v2 demonstrate strong overall performance. Ablation and interpretability analyses consistently show across the three benchmarks that low standalone predictive utility does not necessarily imply low interaction-conditioned predictive utility: a modality that appears weak in isolation can remain informative through pairwise interactions. These findings highlight the interaction-dependent nature of multimodal utility and suggest that modality utility should be characterized from both standalone and interaction-conditioned perspectives.

查看完整摘要
Cinematic video generation requires both temporal inference from limited inputs (i.e., sparse rather than frame-by-frame guidance) and factor-aware control of character, scene, action, and camera. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) offer useful priors for both capabilities, but their representations are not automatically actionable for video generation: a Video Diffusion Transformer (DiT) may bypass them through native conditioning pathways, and using them does not ensure either factor-aware control or generation-compatible temporal prediction. We propose Factor-Aware Predictive Learning (FAPL), which establishes effective MLLM latent use as a shared prerequisite and then targets the two capabilities with complementary learning signals. First, latent-only generator grounding trains the DiT with a frozen MLLM and connector, strengthening the DiT's use of MLLM latents for generation. Second, for factor-aware control, paired videos specify a target-factor change and non-target preservation, and factor-span replacement trains the DiT to realize this relationship in its output. Third, for temporal inference, text-only and text-plus-complete-video observations share a video target, and the trained DiT is then frozen while the MLLM and connector are optimized through it. This final stage trains limited-input representations for generation while retaining complete-observation reconstruction as a complementary training mode. Extensive experiments demonstrate our consistently better performance on three cinematic video generation tasks (i.e., sparse-to-video, factor-aware editing, and multi-shot generation) over baselines and validate FAPL's contributions to MLLM feature use, temporal control, and factor coordination.

查看完整摘要
Vision-language models (VLMs) struggle with in context learning compared to their textual counterparts. Our preliminary study shows that VLMs rely on textual shortcuts and underutilize the visual demonstrations. We therefore hypothesize that VLMs are insufficiently trained to perform visual in-context learning (vICL). To this end, we construct MIME, a task-agnostic vICL training set without textual shortcuts. We ensure the instruction does not state the rule linking the demonstration images to their answers, so the model must infer it from the visual demonstrations. Fine-tuning on MIME improves vICL accuracy by 8.1 to 18.0 points across three VLMs, preserves single-image performance, and improves multi-image and text only in-context learning. In contrast, stating the rule in the training data sharply reduces this gain. We further propose DTAD (Demonstration Text Attention Dropout), which randomly withholds demonstration labels during training so that the VLM must infer the rule from the demonstration images. While achieving comparable vICL accuracy to fine-tuning on MIME, DTAD yields more consistent attention to the demonstration images. On TrueMICL, Qwen3-VL-8B trained on MIME matches frontier models such as Claude Opus 5, and surpasses them with DTAD. Finally, adding MIME to the instruction-tuning mix of LLaVA 1.5 13B more than doubles its vICL accuracy without hurting single-image abilities, whereas a same-size multi-image set improves it only slightly. These results suggest that vICL data should be a standard component of VLM training.

Lifting vision-language features such as CLIP into 3D Gaussian reconstructions enables open-vocabulary segmentation through natural-language queries. Although CLIP represents a broad range of semantic concepts, the lifted features of individual scenes can exhibit strong directional concentration, while raw cosine similarity does not account for the scene-specific feature distribution. We show that substantial segmentation gains can be obtained by changing how a fixed lifted feature field is compared with text. Our method partially subtracts a shared scene direction from visual features and text prototypes and whitens text prototypes using a fixed reference vocabulary of generic terms. It then uses soft clustering for multiclass prediction and CSLS with a relevance margin against the reference vocabulary for single-query prediction. Beyond reducing directional concentration, scene centering improves class-relative feature separation in every evaluated scene. Across seven lifting methods and four datasets, the resulting query-time calibration consistently improves mean intersection over union in both multiclass and single-query segmentation. These gains require no retraining, source-image re-encoding, or feature re-lifting, demonstrating the value of scene-conditioned comparison for making better use of existing lifted representations.

cs.CVcs.AI
查看完整摘要
Few-step autoregressive video generation enables low-latency streaming, yet prevailing training pipelines separate causal adaptation from distribution matching. Recent representation-based text-to-video methods retain this separation by starting from ODE-initialized checkpoints, even when the target distribution is defined directly by reference videos. We introduce Direct Forcing, a single-stage framework that uses reference videos both as paired examples of how a history continues and as a target population for self-generated rollouts. Causal Continuation Alignment learns paired spatiotemporal correspondence under reference histories, while Rollout Distribution Matching uses maximum mean discrepancy to align the representation distributions of self-generated and reference videos. Together, these complementary signals allow a pretrained video model to learn causal continuation and the target video distribution in a single training stage. Dynamic Gradient Balancing adapts the relative gradient contributions of continuation alignment and distribution matching during training. Starting from bidirectional Wan2.1-1.3B, Direct Forcing learns a four-step autoregressive generator in 250 updates without a separate initialization stage or online diffusion-score supervision, achieving 84.63 VBench Total and 86.01 Quality. Ablation studies further demonstrate the benefits of dynamic gradient balancing and reveal distinct representation preferences: deeper features benefit continuation alignment, whereas intermediate features yield better performance for rollout distribution matching.

查看完整摘要
Diffusion-based audio-visual generation methods typically perform joint denoising of audio and video latents throughout the entire sampling trajectory, repeatedly executing modality-specific denoising branches and cross-modal attention at every timestep. However, we observe that such uniform joint computation is not always necessary. The key is a modality-asymmetric recoverability: audio-side drift introduced by mid-stage caching can be substantially reduced by only a few terminal joint denoising steps, whereas video-side degradation caused by caching leads to persistent loss of motion and temporal details. This asymmetry suggests that inference acceleration should selectively cache recoverable audio-side computation while preserving full video refinement. Motivated by this observation, we propose \textbf{Convergence-Adaptive Audio-Visual Cache (CAVC)}, a training-free modality-asymmetric caching framework. CAVC adopts a sandwich-structured computation allocation: after detecting that audio denoiser outputs enter a cache-tolerant regime, it caches and reuses the audio denoiser output to bypass audio denoising and cross-modal attention in the middle stage, while the video branch continues independent spatio-temporal refinement; a few joint steps are then restored at the end to reduce accumulated cross-modal drift and improve synchronization. Experiments across multiple public benchmarks demonstrate that CAVC achieves 1.10--1.35x wall-clock acceleration, improves several visual dynamics and temporal statistics, and maintains competitive audio fidelity and audio-visual alignment.

2026-10-22 06:40:31 | arXiv:2610.30814v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
World models are increasingly used to reduce environment interaction when post-training vision-language-action (VLA) policies with reinforcement learning. Yet they are typically trained to predict pixels one step ahead, even though their role here is not merely to render visually faithful videos but to produce learning signals from imagined rollouts that improve the policy. We show that this mismatch is substantial: lower pixel MSE can produce worse policy gradients, and a world model trained for policy utility can yield better downstream policies despite 17% higher pixel MSE on the next frame. We propose the Downstream-Return Imagination Learning Loop (DRILL), a bilevel framework that updates the world model to maximize the policy's return after an inner GRPO update on imagined rollouts. DRILL has two instantiations. DRILL-VWI is a closed-form surrogate, requiring no additional simulator rollouts, that reweights prediction errors by policy visitation, the group-relative GRPO advantage, and model confidence. DRILL-IMG is a full meta-gradient method that differentiates through the inner update using truncated Hessian-vector products. We show that DRILL-VWI recovers the local one-step component of the DRILL-IMG meta-gradient under score-compatibility and small-residual assumptions. Across five manipulation simulators and two VLA backbones, DRILL-IMG raises mean success rate by 13.1 points over WMPO and 7.9 points over RLVR-World, and complements RLVR pretraining rather than competing with it. More broadly, we believe video world models for VLA reinforcement learning can be productively trained by the policy gradients they produce, not only by pixel-level prediction.

WovenAnchor Matcher: Specialized Intra- and Inter-Image Context Modeling for Feature Matching
Zhiyang Li, Ruijiang Jin, Thibaut Klenke, Yusuke Sekikawa, Nakamasa Inoue

cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Semi-dense local feature matching commonly aggregates contextual information before coarse-to-fine correspondence estimation. However, intra-image and inter-image aggregation serve different roles: the former should propagate spatial evidence within each image while retaining structured two-dimensional dependencies, whereas the latter should gather information from plausible corresponding regions while limiting noise from unrelated locations. We propose WovenAnchor Matcher (WAM), a semi-dense matching framework based on role-specialized context aggregation. WAM introduces two complementary operators. WovenMamba performs intra-image aggregation through horizontal-then-vertical state-space propagation, allowing vertical updates to operate on horizontally contextualized features and thereby encouraging a structured two-dimensional receptive-field bias. AnchorCrossAttention performs inter-image aggregation by using cross-attention to gather context from plausible corresponding regions across the image pair. It performs this exchange over local representative anchors that aggregate feature evidence within each window. On MegaDepth, WAM achieves 66.1 pose AUC@5$^\circ$, improving on the JamMa baseline by 2.0 AUC points under the same evaluation setup. Ablations show that removing or replacing either specialized component reduces accuracy, providing evidence that intra-image propagation and inter-image retrieval benefit from role-specific aggregation designs. Code and pretrained weights are available at https://github.com/mmaiLab/WovenAnchorMatcher.

2026-10-22 05:11:07 | arXiv:2610.30740v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Shadows are observable consequences of latent physical causes, arising from the interaction between illumination, object geometry, and scene structure. A model that understands shadows should be able not only to read a shadow's direction, but also to infer where a shadow should fall when it is absent, and to judge whether a shadow is physically consistent with the scene. We introduce ShadowBench, built from real photographs, which poses these three questions to vision-language models (VLMs). From each scene, we build a correct-shadow, a shadow-removed, and a human-annotated wrong-direction state. Evaluating 19 open VLMs (2B to 78B) and frontier proprietary models, we find that VLMs read a visible shadow's direction well above chance and improve with scale, yet infer the absent direction only weakly and fall to chance at judging physical consistency even under a bias-free two-alternative test that removes the yes-bias of single-image naturalness questions. Only Gemini-3.1-pro exceeds chance on validity, and even then only modestly and far below humans. Chain-of-Thought (CoT) does not help. The missing ingredient is not reasoning but knowledge that image generation models (IGMs) already hold. Routing a VLM through an IGM lifts its inference well above its direct attempt and helps it flag the wrong-direction shadows it otherwise accepts. Cast-shadow direction is a single facet of illumination understanding, but the sharp gap it reveals between VLMs and IGMs suggests image-generation or reconstruction objectives as a route toward illumination-aware VLMs.

查看完整摘要
Video provides a natural source of supervision: what happens next serves as a learning signal for visual world modeling. Yet masked video pretraining focuses on recovering missing content from surrounding context rather than predicting the future from the past. Autoregressive methods instead predict forward one token at a time, but have yet to match joint-embedding methods in representation quality. This gap motivates us to revisit the granularity and space of future prediction. In this work, we introduce VANE, a self-supervised visual pretraining framework built on next-frame prediction in embedding space. A causal encoder summarizes past observations for a state predictor that infers the representation of the entire next frame, making temporal evolution the primary learning signal. This prediction is carried out in a fixed embedding space provided by a frozen semantic tokenizer, emphasizing scene content and structure. To retain fine-grained spatial detail alongside semantic content, a lightweight auxiliary generative decoder reconstructs future-frame patches conditioned on the predicted representations. To unify visual modeling across images and videos, we construct a local-to-global crop sequence for still images, allowing the same encoder to predict newly revealed regions from earlier crops under the same objective. Experiments show that VANE improves with model and data scaling, achieving SOTA on most anticipation, recognition, and dense perception benchmarks among unified encoders. Especially on EPIC-KITCHENS-100 action anticipation, it surpasses V-JEPA 2.1 by 7.1 points in mean-class recall@5. These results demonstrate that next-frame prediction in embedding space is effective and scalable for visual world modeling.

Training-free few-step samplers are often described as nearly quality-lossless under conventional FID/CLIP-style evaluation. It remains unclear whether that conclusion transfers to more realistic and complex generation-quality benchmarks. We first systematize the quality metrics used to evaluate training-free sampling acceleration for diffusion and flow-matching models. We find that representative training-free step-shortening methods often remain nearly lossless under FID/CLIP-class metrics, yet can suffer substantial quality degradation when evaluated with semantic-composition, dense-instruction, and long-text-rendering benchmarks such as GenEval, DPG-Bench, and LongTextBench. 2026-10-22 03:00:34 | arXiv:2610.30639v1 |
cs.CV
查看完整摘要
Self-supervised Vision Transformers learn strong image-level representations, but their patch-level representations can be unreliable for dense prediction. We study this mismatch and find that the class token concentrates global semantics on a small number of representative patches, while dense prediction errors often occur on low-attention regions. We provide a local gradient analysis showing that, along the [CLS] attention readout, image-level self-distillation gradients are weighted by [CLS]-to-patch attention mass. To provide masked patches with global semantics without changing the architecture, we propose Cross Distillation (CODI), which mixes a small amount of the teacher class-token distribution into each teacher patch-token target. CODI gives every masked patch an attention-independent semantic correction while preserving the standard self-distillation architecture. Experiments on dense and image-level benchmarks show that CODI improves both dense and image-level performance under aligned pretraining settings. Code is available at https://github.com/EldercatSAM/CODI.

Review-oriented workflows have become the dominant approach to AI-assisted document review in electronic discovery (eDiscovery). How to validate the effectiveness of review-oriented workflows has, however, remained a confusing and controversial topic in technology-assisted review (TAR) in the law. We examine the sources of this controversy, and propose a simple and practical evaluation approach for review-oriented workflows. Our Delayed Countdown method is easily incorporated into existing review-oriented workflows and software, is compatible with arbitrary stopping rules, and produces valid frequentist confidence intervals on effectiveness. We explain the conceptual foundations of the method, provide example computations, discuss the handling of logically impossible recall values, and suggest future work on decreasing review costs.

Over-representation analysis (ORA) is widely used to interpret gene-expression studies, but its outputs are often fragmented across gene-set databases and presented as flat term lists that obscure gene-level links among related enrichment signals. We propose Graph-Based Over-Representation Retrieval-Augmented Generation (GBOR-RAG), an integrated and traceable framework for ORA interpretation. GBOR-RAG represents heterogeneous gene-set databases as a unified term--gene knowledge graph, where shared genes connect related terms across sources. After graph-based ORA scoring, the framework retrieves a compact connected evidence subgraph that preserves both cross-database relationships and gene-level support. The subgraph conditions an LLM to generate biological interpretations, and we introduce a claim-level faithfulness metric to assess whether generated claims are supported by explicit evidence. Experiments show that graph-based ORA preserves conventional ORA results with high consistency (mean Pearson $r=0.997$) while reducing query time by a median of $37\times$ (up to $48\times$). The retrieved subgraphs average 36.8 nodes and achieve the highest overall integration score (4.12), and GBOR-RAG attains the highest claim-level faithfulness (0.90) among the compared systems. Adding a PubMed literature layer further improves the overall interpretation quality from 4.17 to 4.75.

This paper introduces Journal Basket, a browser-based tool developed to help business students conduct journal-bounded literature searches and currently available in beta. While monolingual language model (LM) surprisal is widely established as a predictor of reading times, it cannot capture the non-native (L2) reading dynamics that are shaped by the reader's first language (L1). In this work, we investigate whether the cross-lingual transfer within bilingual models aligns with human L1-driven biases during L2 reading. We evaluate bilingual models on eye-tracking data of readers from 12 diverse L1 backgrounds reading English texts. To isolate L1-specific transfer, we compare bilingual models trained on an L1 that matches the readers' L1 background to both a monolingual English baseline and bilingual models trained on other L1s. We demonstrate that matching the readers' L1 improves the prediction of L2 reading times. This predictive advantage is concentrated in late-stage eye-tracking measures, modulated by L1-L2 script overlap, and peaks among intermediate-proficiency readers. These findings provide evidence that bilingual LMs capture aspects of cross-linguistic influence, and that such influence is present in L2 reading behavior.

查看完整摘要
Purpose: To describe an evidence-aware retrieval evaluation protocol for a clinical retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) system over breast lesion management literature, addressing the dependence of retrieval scores on annotation coverage. Methods: An 18-document corpus and an 18-query development benchmark, with one Yes/No or WH-question per document, were used. Retrieval combined dense embeddings, BM25, and cross-encoder reranking. Chunk sizes of 128 to 1024 tokens, overlaps of 0 to 20%, and four retriever configurations were evaluated. Relevance was defined by evidence spans mapped to all containing chunks, treating overlapping chunks with equivalent evidence as relevant. Retrieval was evaluated using R-Precision, Recall@K, MRR, and nDCG@K. Separately, generation was assessed on a 512-token index at K=1 to 5 using DeepEval claim-level metrics, with deepseek-v4-pro as the LLM judge, and BLEU/ROUGE metrics. Results: The selected hybrid retriever, using 384-token chunks and 20% overlap, retrieved accepted evidence in the top three results for 17 of 18 queries. Recall@3, R-Precision, MRR@3, and nDCG@3 each reached 0.94. Ground-truth curation increased evidence spans from 25 to 37 and accepted chunk targets from 36 to 51 without changing retrieval rankings. Conclusion: Cross-encoder reranking achieved the highest retrieval scores. Expanded relevance annotations increased recognized evidence without changing rankings, highlighting annotation sensitivity. The small development set and retrieval-informed curation require independent validation, including radiologist assessment of evidence sufficiency.

Accuracy on medical examinations is common evidence offered for the clinical use of language models, yet a health system that intends to run an open model on its own hardware needs to know considerably more: whether the model recognizes when it is likely to be wrong, whether medical fine-tuning improves this ability, whether its correct answers rest on sound clinical reasoning, or whether it actually uses the images it is given. We examine these questions for four open models of 8 to 27 billion parameters on the Spanish MIR residency examination, scoring 1,552 questions from eight editions under the exam's own rules. Answering every question, the three larger models would rank among roughly the first 600 to 3,000 candidates, with 41 to 43 specialties still open to them. Abstention, however, never pays, because even their least confident answers are right more often than the 25% break-even. Medical fine-tuning reduced overconfidence at the cost of accuracy, without improving how well confidence separates right from wrong answers. Moreover, 13-38% of correct answers rejected a distractor for a clinically unsound reason that confidence did not reflect, and removing or swapping the provided contextual images left accuracy unchanged.

面向困惑度感知黑盒攻击的扩散语言模型

1/10
Diffusion Language Models for Perplexity-Aware Black-Box Attacks
Ali Al-Lawati, Elsayed Eshra, Dongwon Lee, Suhang Wang
个性化推荐理由:

该论文聚焦于利用扩散语言模型进行黑盒攻击与困惑度感知，属于安全/对抗攻击范畴，与我的核心关注点（推荐、搜索、广告系统及支撑它们的LLM/Transformer技术）无直接关联。其主题更偏向NLP安全而非排序或推荐建模，因此相关性极低。

2026-10-22 17:00:39 | arXiv:2610.31558v1 |
cs.LGcs.CLstat.ML
查看完整摘要
\emph{Continuous-space} attacks find prompts that trigger malicious behaviors, such as overthinking, in black-box LLMs by sampling or optimizing over a low-dimensional Gaussian latent space and decoding it to text through a \emph{random mapping} and nearest-token lookup. Because the random mapping is not learned from language, its prompts can be highly unnatural, and as such easily detectable through input guardrails, and the risk estimates they yield are not grounded in natural prompts. In this paper, we demonstrate that the same continuous latent-space framework can be coupled directly to a learned generative distribution over language, a continuous diffusion language model (dLLM), which removes the artificial random mapping while preserving the standard-Gaussian latent formulation and the downstream sampling or optimization procedure. We evaluate this approach under two settings: sampling-based estimation using \emph{RareTrap} and optimization-based search using \emph{ThinkTrap}. We find that character-level perplexity drops from $10^3$--$10^7$ to 3--5 in RareTrap and from 20--28 to 4.3--5.4 in ThinkTrap. We also find that model risk estimates can differ by an order of magnitude across the two mappings.

Text-to-speech (TTS) is usually evaluated by transcribing its output with an automatic speech recognition (ASR) system, so the word error rate also counts recognizer errors. We measure these errors on human recordings of the test sentences. On LibriSpeech and FLEURS, the evaluation recognizer misses 33-67% of very rare words even in human speech, and all three recognizers miss 22-42%. Checking every word with two more recognizer families lowers the apparent TTS error on words that at least one recognizer hears two- to three-fold (very rare LibriSpeech words: from 10-13% to 3-5%), yet very rare words remain the hardest. Ignoring words the recognizer misses in human speech is biased: the other recognizers find TTS errors far more often on these words (4-6% vs. 0.2-0.3%) and overturn about half of the errors this mask keeps. As half of the remaining adjudicated errors may be spelling variants, a listening test is needed.

As wearable and digital health systems increasingly generate longitudinal physiological records, communicating these observations in patient-accessible language remains a significant challenge. Large Language Models (LLMs) offer a promising mechanism for translating structured clinical information into readable narratives, but this process may introduce semantic drift, where clinically precise meaning is distorted during simplification. Bashkir is a low-resource agglutinative language written in Cyrillic and remains poorly represented in modern LLMs. We present an open resource suite and a systematic benchmark of adaptation methods: the Bashkir Web Corpus (71,567 documents, 46.9M tokens, 16 sources) and three labelled datasets for binary, multiclass and multilabel text classification; the benchmark covers the first two. Experiments use eight architectures (82M-8B: DistilGPT2, GPT-2 base/medium, Phi-3-mini, Mistral-7B, YandexGPT-5-Lite-8B, mBERT, XLM-R) and three adaptation groups (full fine-tuning, QLoRA, in-context learning), plus continual pretraining (CPT). (1) QLoRA on 3.8-8B models outperforms encoder-only fine-tuning on binary classification: YandexGPT-5-Lite-8B with CPT reaches macro-F1 0.748 vs 0.653 (XLM-R) and 0.650 (mBERT); pairwise differences are significant after Holm correction (p<0.001). (2) YandexGPT-5-Lite-8B significantly outperforms Mistral-7B and Phi-3-mini on multiclass classification, BPB and native-speaker ratings (mean 3.28 vs 2.52 and 2.22; n=100; alpha=0.69-0.77; p<0.05); on binary classification its QLoRA advantage is not significant (p>=0.084). (3) CPT effects depend on model and task: perplexity improves for Phi-3-mini (+0.58) and YandexGPT-5-Lite-8B (+0.33) but worsens for Mistral-7B (-1.15, p<0.001); on binary classification CPT helps Mistral-7B most (+4.0 macro-F1). (4) Tokeniser fertility on Bashkir varies 2.67x (2.92-7.80) and tracks adaptation quality, while perplexity across tokenisers is not comparable. Cyrillic tokenisers with Russian pretraining are preferable; LLM judges reproduce native ratings for grammar and coherence (rho=0.33-0.46) but not language correctness (rho=0.16, p=0.11); native evaluation remains necessary. All artefacts are open-licensed.
